# Agent network design

> **Status (2026-10-14):** design, with the runtime core, the renderer service, the regression harness and the frontend built, and both services deployed on 2026-10-11: `anyplot-renderer` and `anyplot-agents` run in Cloud Run (europe-west4) behind the `deploy-renderer` and `deploy-agents` triggers, and the API side switched on the same day for admins (the `AGENT_USER_ID_KEY` secret is bound, the deploy-api trigger sets `_AGENT_SERVICE_URL` and `_AGENT_ENABLED=true`, the deploy-app trigger builds the chat page in). Built: the `agents/` package (ADK pin, `AgentSettings` and the Pydantic contracts in `agents/anyplot/schemas.py`); the deterministic data layer in `agents/anyplot/data/` (the parser, data roles, default bindings and the dataset store described under [Parse](#parse)) and `bindings.apply` (`session_state.apply_bindings`, called by `PUT /v1/sessions/{sid}/bindings` and the `set_bindings` tool); the session language (one per session, English by default, set by the first typed message or by the user through `PUT /v1/sessions/{sid}/language` and the root's `set_language` tool; see [Session language](#session-language)); the deterministic code layer in `agents/anyplot/code/` (the two-profile AST validator in `validate.py`, plus the protected regions, the canvas normaliser, the readiness scan, the edit applier, the loader and the export in `regions.py`, `normalise.py`, `readiness.py`, `edits.py`, `loader.py` and `export.py`); the runtime core (the root agent, the adapters and the reviewer, since 2026-10-13 with the catalogue rubric, a self-review before the first review (in every round until 2026-10-14) and the best-scored version shipped, the plot pipeline, the ScopeGuard, Budget and ToolSafety plugins, the render layer with the probe harness, the host gates (the G10 glyph gate since 2026-10-12) and the `fake` and `local` Docker backends, the private `/v1` service with its `anyplot/1` stream, the run queue in front of whole pipeline runs, one-theme renders with an on-demand theme toggle, and since 2026-10-14 the root's `run_data_code`, which runs a data-generating snippet through the renderer's `POST /run-data` (see [Two steps: data first, then the plot](#two-steps-data-first-then-the-plot)); see `agents/README.md`); the service's image `agents/Dockerfile`, its Cloud Build config `agents/cloudbuild.yaml` and its CI image job (see [Serving and infrastructure](#serving-and-infrastructure)); the `anyplot-renderer` service in `agents/renderer/` (the sandbox executor with the kill path, the run watchdog and the launcher retry from spikes S and S2, one render slot, cancellation on disconnect, the caller check, its image with the multi-script fallback fonts (Noto Sans, Noto Sans CJK and 17 script and symbol families, which the harness appends to every font lookup, since 2026-10-12), its CI image job and its Cloud Build config) and the `remote` render backend that calls it (see [Render](#render)); the model-regression harness `agents/evals/matrix.py` with the 120 synthetic spike-X cases its seeded generator `agents/evals/make_fixtures.py` writes, the 23 stress cases of `agents/evals/make_stress.py` and their model-free static stage (`--static`, see [Bounds](#bounds)), the blind two-run review gallery and the catalogue eligibility sweep `agents/evals/eligibility.py` (see [Model-version regression harness](#model-version-regression-harness)), with the Claude Haiku 5.5 baseline from the rerun of spike X on main in the evening of 2026-10-10 committed in `agents/evals/baselines/`; the `/debug/agent` BFF router in `api/routers/agent.py` (shipped dark behind `AGENT_ENABLED`); the frontend (the chat page `app/src/pages/AgentChatPage.tsx` with the result card, the stream parser `app/src/lib/sse.ts` and the session hook `app/src/hooks/useAgentSession.ts`, the `.adapt()` button on the plot page, and the analytics events; built only with `VITE_ENABLE_AGENT_CHAT=true`, and driven end to end against the local BFF mock `app/scripts/agent-bff-mock.mjs`); the footer strip on every PNG the service serves (`agents/anyplot/render/watermark.py`, drawn with the vendored JetBrains Mono, see [Footer strip](#footer-strip)); and two shared building blocks, `core/canvas.py` (the canvas gate and the PNG auto-reject checks) and `core/defects.py` (the review feedback grammar). Every agent runs on Claude Haiku 5.5 on Vertex AI by default, with Gemini 3.8 Flash as the second arm behind `AGENT_PROVIDER` (see the Model row under [Goals and fixed decisions](#goals-and-fixed-decisions)). Not built: `core/catalogue`, the Gemini baseline, the second stage of the judge-only scope eval (`agents/evals/scope.py`, built and first run on 2026-10-10) through the root, the flow evals, `agents-eval.yml`, and everything in phase 2. The research behind it was verified against ADK v2.11.0 and the Vertex AI documentation on 2026-10-08; re-check version-sensitive facts (model ids, prices, ADK APIs) before you implement a section.

This document describes the agent network that lets a visitor paste their own data on a plot page and get that plot adapted, rendered and reviewed ("Use with my data"), and later helps them find the right plot type. It is built with [Google ADK](https://adk.dev) (Python) on Vertex AI (now branded Gemini Enterprise Agent Platform) inside the `anyplot` GCP project: on Claude Haiku 5.5 by default, with Gemini 3.8 Flash as the second arm.

## Goals and fixed decisions

| Topic | Decision |
|---|---|
| Product step 1 | "Use with my data": the user pastes data on a plot page, the network adapts the catalogue implementation for that spec and library to the data, renders it in a sandbox, reviews it with at most one repair round (a render repaired after a rejection is reviewed once more), and returns the image and the code. For measurements only, the eval switch `AGENT_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (`--max-attempts` of the harness) can raise this to three attempts, a second repair round; the default stays one repair round, and a run makes at most two reviewer calls either way |
| Product step 2 | "Find the right plot type", built on the catalogue knowledge that also backs the MCP server |
| Result delivery | Users see and copy both the image and the code: the plot is shown inline in the theme it was rendered in (since 2026-11-01 the site theme the page shows, unless the user asked for a theme in the chat), a light and dark switch renders the other theme on demand without any model call, and the image can be copied to the clipboard and downloaded as PNG; the code is shown with syntax highlighting, copied with one click, and downloaded together with `data.csv` so it runs unchanged |
| Plot attribution | Owner decision of 2026-10-10 (variant `s1c-jb-sq2` of the comparison): every PNG the service serves carries a footer strip below the plot, "made with any.plot()" on the left and "anyplot.ai/<spec-id>" on the right, in JetBrains Mono with the dot drawn as a brand-green square. It is composited at image level after the run and never written into the user's code, so the exported `plot.py` reproduces the plot without it. MonoLisa, the site's own font, is not used for it, because its EULA covers desktop, web, application and ePub use only. See [Footer strip](#footer-strip) |
| Render scope | Python first, designed so every further library is additive. Phase 1 serves matplotlib and seaborn. The runtime registry also has runtimes for plotnine and altair since 2026-10-25, for pygal and lets-plot since 2026-10-26 (steps 1b and 1c of [the phase-2 plan](../../agentic/specs/261024-agent-all-libraries.md)), for ggplot2 on the R runtime since 2026-10-29 (step 2c), for Makie on the Julia runtime since 2026-10-30 (step 5), for Chart.js, D3, ECharts and Highcharts on the JavaScript runtime since 2026-10-30 (JS-4a of step 3), for MUI X on it since 2026-10-31 (JS-4c), and for plotly and bokeh on the Python runtime since 2026-10-31 (PB-2a of step 4, through the renderer's figure mode): all 15 catalogue libraries. `AGENT_LIBRARIES` still defaults to `matplotlib,seaborn`; the deploy serves every library with a runtime (the six Python libraries since 2026-10-28, ggplot2 since 2026-10-29, Makie, Chart.js, D3, ECharts and Highcharts since 2026-10-30, MUI X, plotly and bokeh since 2026-10-31), because only the admin reaches the chat yet (owner decision), and each library's eval matrix at 80 % gates the public release. Highcharts is served under its non-commercial licence (owner decision of 2026-10-30): the site charges nothing |
| Data input | Paste text only (CSV, TSV, semicolon CSV, a table aligned with spaces, JSON including API answers that wrap their records, a Python literal), hard cap 1 MB (200 KB until 2026-10-14); data the assistant computes (`run_data_code`, `generate_data`, `prepare_data`) up to 2 MB |
| Access | Visible only in local development and for admins, gated exactly like `/debug/*`; not public in phase 1 |
| Guardrail scope | Allowed: choosing plots, the user's data for them, adapting, rendering and styling, and questions about catalogue code (styling, exports). Everything else gets a fixed refusal |
| Runtime | Two Cloud Run services of their own: `anyplot-agents` runs the agents, and `anyplot-renderer` runs the code in Cloud Run sandboxes (owner decision of 2026-10-10, so a local `adk web` and the deployed agents service share one renderer). The managed Vertex code-execution sandbox is Python/JS only with a fixed library set (no kaleido, altair, plotnine, pygal, lets-plot, R, Julia, or Chromium), so it cannot render the catalogue |
| Model | Owner decision of 2026-10-09: Claude Haiku 5.5 (`claude-haiku-5-5`) on Vertex AI, location `eu`, is the phase-1 default for every agent and for the scope and dataset judge (`AGENT_PROVIDER=anthropic-vertex`). Gemini 3.8 Flash (`gemini-3.8-flash`, with the judge on `gemini-3.5-flash-lite`) stays the second arm behind `AGENT_PROVIDER=gemini`, so the two can be compared on price and quality. The judge follows the provider: `AGENT_JUDGE_MODEL` must be a model of the same family. Every model id is an exact pin, never an alias such as `gemini-flash-latest`: that alias is undocumented on Vertex, its served version is unobservable, and it returned 404 once when its target retired. "3.8" in this document always means the Gemini model, never a Python version |
| Python | Python 3.14 everywhere, the same as the pipeline |
| Location | The `eu` multi-region from day one. No Gemini 3.x Flash is served in europe-west4; `eu` keeps ML processing in the EU at +10 % price and is compatible with the privacy page's EU claim |
| Language | Owner decision of 2026-10-12: one session language for the replies and the first plot's text (title, axis titles, legend entries, annotations), English by default like the whole site, also when the browser is set to another language. A paste before any typed message sets it to the language of its notes or column names, the first typed message to the language it is written in, and a choice from the chat header's chip or a request in the chat sets it for good. Owner decision of 2026-11-01: a plot keeps its text's language after a follow-up message in another language; only a request for the plot's text, or the chip, changes it (see [Session language](#session-language)). Code, code comments, the `changes` notes and the root's `change_request` to the adapter stay English; column names, units and category labels from the data keep the user's spelling in every language |
| Quick feedback | From phase 1, one click plus a short text on any result submits the whole case (conversation, code, images, data profile, optionally the data file, model and prompt config) for tuning; opt-in for real users later |
| Model-version testing | From phase 1, a regression harness so a newer model version, the other provider's arm, or a prompt change is one command or one workflow dispatch with a diff report against the pinned baseline |
| Catalogue normalisation | A parallel pipeline workstream makes catalogue code data-derived and canvas-correct at the source |
| Licensing | Everything stays open under MIT in this repository, `agents/` included; copyability is accepted. Selling AI credits to people who create plots often remains a later option. The sister project's "open core" reserves learned data rather than code, and this design does the equivalent: user data, feedback cases and promoted eval cases live only in the private bucket, never in git, so the code stays MIT as the legal page says. The one third-party asset in the tree is JetBrains Mono 2.304 for the footer strip, vendored unmodified under the SIL Open Font License 1.1 with its licence and a notice in `agents/anyplot/render/fonts/`; the OFL allows bundling it with MIT code, and the legal page's typography row and its "entire codebase" sentence get the font and the qualifier before non-admin use (owner task). The renderer image also installs fonts from Debian's `fonts-noto-core` and `fonts-noto-cjk` packages (SIL Open Font License 1.1, their copyright notices copied into the image under `/usr/share/doc/fonts-noto/`); they are not vendored in the tree, the OFL allows bundling them in the image with those notices, and it places no restriction on the PNGs rendered with them. Before the first paid credit, the Highcharts licence must be settled (its free licence excludes platforms with direct or indirect revenue): buy one or keep Highcharts out of paid features. Source-available licences (FSL, BUSL, PolyForm) and AGPL were assessed on 2026-10-09 and rejected |

## What the research settled

- **ADK 2.11.0, 2.x idioms only.** `from google.adk import Agent, Workflow, Event, Context`; plugins on `App(plugins=[...])`; sub-agent modes `task` and `single_turn`; a `Workflow` with a Pydantic `input_schema` passed in `tools=[...]` becomes a NodeTool whose progress events reach the stream. `SequentialAgent`, `LoopAgent` and `ParallelAgent` are deprecated, `AgentTool` is discouraged (its events never reach the stream), `global_instruction` is deprecated, and `GOOGLE_GENAI_USE_ENTERPRISE=TRUE` replaces the Vertex flag. `abort_signal` is a `Runner.run_async` keyword argument, not a `RunConfig` field. `instruction` strings get `{var}` state templating (a missing key raises); `static_instruction` is sent unchanged.
- **ADK's own server is never exposed.** `adk web`, `adk api_server` and `get_fast_api_app` are unauthenticated and let the client choose the user id and write state, events and artifacts. The network embeds `Runner.run_async` in its own FastAPI routes.
- **No agent gets a `code_executor`.** ADK auto-runs the first fenced code block the model emits. Rendering is a deterministic tool that calls Cloud Run sandboxes (`gcloud beta run deploy --sandbox-launcher`, gen2, Preview, no extra cost): `sandbox do` has no egress, no environment, secrets or metadata server, and a read-only root filesystem. Region availability and Chromium, R and Julia inside the sandbox are undocumented, so a spike gates phase 1.
- **Adapting catalogue code is not a data swap.** 172 of 325 matplotlib files set axis limits with data-scale literals, 87 place annotations at numeric data coordinates, literal statistics are common, and 137 matplotlib and 138 seaborn files already fail the 3200x1800 canvas gate (`bbox_inches='tight'`). A pure swap passes the structural gates in 40 of 63 runs and 0 of 7 for date axes. Structured search-and-replace hunks of about 100 to 180 output tokens passed all gates in a proxy test. Inline data literals are infeasible beyond a few hundred rows, so data travels as a sidecar `data.csv`.
- **Catalogue access is in-process.** The six FastMCP tools in `api/mcp/server.py` are plain async functions, but they raise `ValueError` (which aborts an ADK run unless handled), and over HTTP every result reaches the model twice. Their category-scoped AND search, `# noqa` stripping and slimmer payloads (no review internals) landed on 2026-10-09, so the in-process wrappers only need error handling and fencing.
- **Infra constraints.** `anyplot-api` is a single Cloud Run instance (1 vCPU, 1 GiB, concurrency 40) running as the default compute service account with `roles/editor`; `anyplot-images` is a public bucket; `aiplatform.googleapis.com` is not enabled; the API image has no plotting extras, R, Julia, Node or Chromium.
- **Privacy.** The privacy policy in `app/src/pages/LegalPage.tsx` says the AI never sees a visitor's request and that everything runs in europe-west4. The owner-only phase 1 is compatible. Any non-owner use needs a legal-page PR, a retention design, and the owner's answer on the GCP agreement type (abuse-monitoring prompt logging for up to 90 days under the standard terms).
- **Cost on `eu` with Gemini 3.8 Flash (now the second arm).** List price $1.65 input and $8.25 output per 1M tokens; a 50 % credit makes the effective price $0.825 and $4.125 through 2026-12-31. The judge model costs $0.33 and $2.75. Spike X measured a median of $0.124 per passed plot at list price on 2026-10-10 ($0.062 at the promo price), above the $0.05 line at either price, against $0.0041 for Claude Haiku 5.5; a model of the adapter truncation fix from that run keeps the Gemini median near $0.125. Spend caps count list price, not the promo. Gemini 3.8 Flash is in the short-term availability tier (no retirement date yet, at least 45 days' notice) and not on the Standard PayGo tier list, so expect 429s and plan a model bump gated by evals.

## Architecture

```
Browser ──fetchWithAuth──► anyplot-api  /debug/agent/*   (require_admin → AdminIdentity, CSRF header, AGENT_ENABLED kill switch)
                             │ ID token (roles/run.invoker) + X-Anyplot-User + X-Request-Id
                             ▼
                       anyplot-agents  (Cloud Run gen2, --no-allow-unauthenticated, own service account)
                         FastAPI /v1/* → message limits, closed session → Runner (at once, no queue)
                           → Runner(app=App(plugins=[ScopeGuard, Budget, ToolSafety, ContextFilter(6)]))
                           root "anyplot" ── session tools ── plot_pipeline (Workflow NodeTool, input_schema=PipelineArgs)
                                            run_data_code, plot_pipeline → run queue (1 in flight, 1 start a minute, 600 s maximum wait)
                                @node run_pipeline:  adapter → apply + validate → render, one theme → gates
                                                     → reviewer (≤1) → repair (≤1) → Event(output=PlotResult)
                         POST /v1/.../versions/{v}/render → render the other theme → gates   (no model call, no queue)
                             │ every render: `remote` backend, ID token (roles/run.invoker)        adk web on a developer machine
                             ▼                                                                       │ the same `remote` backend
                       anyplot-renderer  (Cloud Run gen2, --sandbox-launcher, --no-allow-unauthenticated, zero-role account)  ◄┘
                         FastAPI POST /render → one render slot → per theme: sandbox do (gVisor, no egress) → raw outputs
```

Only one agent talks to the user. Everything the research shows an LLM does not improve (parsing, default bindings, the data loader, the canvas, safety checks, export) is plain code.

### Agents

| Agent | Kind | Model and thinking | Instructions | Tools | Output |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `anyplot` (root) | `Agent` with no `mode` (chat root); the only user-facing agent | `AGENT_MODEL` (claude-haiku-5-5; gemini-3.8-flash on the Gemini arm), effort `medium` since 2026-10-13 (`low` before) with adaptive thinking on Claude (`AGENT_ROOT_THINKING_BUDGET`, 1,024 tokens added to the cap, since 2026-10-11), `thinking_level=LOW` on Gemini, `max_output_tokens` 4096 plus the thinking budget | `static_instruction` from `agents/anyplot/prompts/root.md`: scope list, fixed-refusal rule, session-language rule, tool rules, the "never" list. A session block adds only server-validated values (the session language's source, and its value inside a `<plot_language>` fence because a chosen language is free text; the spec id, library, dataset status, whether bindings are complete, plot versions); catalogue text such as the spec title never enters it, because that block has instruction priority, and the root reads it fenced through `get_spec_brief`. The root's `before_model_callback` appends the block to the system instruction as a second part after the static policy, and on Claude each part is a system block of its own with the cache breakpoint on the first. Since 2026-10-15 it never stands in the user's turn: as an ADK `instruction` next to a `static_instruction`, ADK 2.11 sent it there between `<<<BEGIN_SYSTEM_INSTRUCTION>>>` markers, and the root's thinking took it for user text that forged a system instruction in 7 of 8 prep-eval turns | `get_dataset_profile`, `get_spec_brief`, `get_current_code(version)`, `set_bindings`, `set_language(language)` (the session language, validated by the server like `set_bindings`, since 2026-10-12), `prepare_data(operations)` (the step-1 data operations, applied by the server), `generate_data(variables, series)` (a table the server computes from formulas, since 2026-10-11), `run_data_code(code, source_language)` (the user's data-generating snippet, translated to Python by the root and run in the renderer's sandbox, since 2026-10-14), `plot_pipeline`. Phase 2 adds `find_specs`, `get_spec_knowledge`, `select_spec` | Chat text |
| `adapter_<library>` | `Agent(mode="single_turn")`, one per enabled library, run with `ctx.run_node` inside the pipeline, `include_contents='none'` | `AGENT_MODEL`, effort `high` since 2026-10-13 (`medium` before) with adaptive thinking on Claude (`AGENT_ADAPTER_THINKING_BUDGET`, added to the caps since 2026-10-11; 8,192 tokens since 2026-10-14, 2,048 before), `thinking_level=MEDIUM` on Gemini; `max_output_tokens` 40,960 on Claude for edit-only and full-file calls since 2026-10-30 (with the thinking room 49,152, about 143 s at most, and the Vertex client's own 300 s timeout, because the Anthropic SDK refuses a non-streaming request that large otherwise), and on Gemini 8192 for edit-only calls (thinking tokens count toward the cap, and a call cut off at 8192 still leaves the repair its 75 s of the soft deadline) and 12288 when a full file is allowed, with `thinking_level=LOW` there; every cap leaves ample headroom above the largest measured answer (see `agents/README.md`) | `static_instruction` (never `instruction`, because the verbatim prompt files contain `{THEME}`, `{lang}` and `{lib}` braces that ADK would template): `prompts/adapter.md` with its slots filled by the library's language (`adapter_python.md` or `adapter_r.md`, since 2026-10-29) plus `prompts/default-style-guide.md` and `prompts/library/<lib>.md` verbatim, about 8k to 10k tokens; a cached prefix on Claude through the App's `ContextCacheConfig` (5-minute lifetime), and above the 6,144-token implicit-cache minimum of Gemini 3.8 Flash | none | `AdaptPlan` |
| `reviewer` | `Agent(mode="single_turn")`, no tools; its `before_model_callback` appends the PNG of the rendered theme as ordinary user content after a label that names the theme (on Gemini at `media_resolution=MEDIUM`, 560 tokens), loaded by `render_id` from the render store, never from state | `AGENT_MODEL`, effort `high` with adaptive thinking on Claude since 2026-10-13 (`AGENT_REVIEWER_THINKING_BUDGET`, 4,096 tokens added to the cap since 2026-10-14; effort `low` without thinking before), LOW on Gemini, `max_output_tokens` 8192 on both providers (4096 before 2026-10-13) | Since 2026-10-13 (`AGENT_REVIEWER_MODE=catalogue`, the default): the offline scorer's catalogue rubric (`prompts/reviewer_catalogue.md` before `scoring.system_prompt`), answered as a `ScoreAnswer` whose points the code turns into the verdict (`AGENT_REVIEW_BAR`, `AGENT_STATUS_BAR`) and whose defect lines feed the repair; the measurements behind it are in `agents/README.md`, "The plot pipeline since 2026-10-13". Before (`checklist`): `static_instruction` from `prompts/reviewer.md`: a reduced checklist (VQ-01, VQ-02, VQ-03, VQ-06, VQ-07, SC-01, SC-03, DQ-03 for stale claims, AR-09 for clipping), the session language as `plot_language`, against which VQ-06 judges the plot's own text (fail-open: names and labels from the data never fail it), the theme-readability and defect-grammar sections adapted from `prompts/workflow-prompts/ai-quality-review.md`, and the style guide | none | `catalogue` mode (the default): `ScoreAnswer{items[], defects[], suggestions[], description}`, the points per criterion and defect lines in the `DEFECT_RE` grammar, read by `scoring.parse_answer`. `checklist` mode: `Verdict{ok, defects[]}` with fixed ids, rendered into the existing `DEFECT_RE` grammar on the server; an image defect is filed under the rendered theme whatever theme the reviewer named, a `code` defect stays `code` |
| Scope judge (not an agent) | A direct model call inside `ScopeGuardPlugin`, client built by `make_judge_client()`: an `AsyncAnthropicVertex` client answering under a structured-output format (`output_config.format`) on Claude, `genai.Client(enterprise=True, project=..., location=settings.location)` on Gemini | `AGENT_JUDGE_MODEL` (claude-haiku-5-5; gemini-3.5-flash-lite on the Gemini arm), JSON schema, at most 256 output tokens, 4 s budget with one retry after a 0.5 s back-off, on Gemini its own safety filters off | `prompts/scope_judge.md` | none | `{verdict: in_scope|out_of_scope|attack|needs_data|other_library, lang}` |

The root's "never" list, enforced by the stream translator and the evals: never write or run code (code changes happen only through `plot_pipeline`; code answers are short prose that references lines from `get_current_code`; the translator strips fenced code blocks longer than 10 lines); never invent spec ids; never paste data rows (naming a single value the user asks about is fine); never emit links (a scheme or `www.`; a bare web address such as `jana-novak.de` is data), HTML or markdown images; never claim success unless `PlotResult.status` is `ok` or `needs_attention`.

Rules for every agent:

- Models come only from `make_model()` in `agents/anyplot/models.py`, which builds the model of the configured provider. On `anthropic-vertex` (the default) it returns `VertexClaude`, ADK's `Claude` with an `AsyncAnthropicVertex` client built from the settings, adaptive thinking on every kind (`AGENT_ROOT_THINKING_BUDGET`, 1,024, `AGENT_ADAPTER_THINKING_BUDGET`, 8,192, and `AGENT_REVIEWER_THINKING_BUDGET`, 4,096, each at most 8,192 and added to that agent's output cap; 0 turns it off, and the eval harness overrides the root's and the adapter's with `--thinking-budget`; a replayed thinking block whose signature no longer matches the conversation, which happens when a tool changes the session block between the root's calls, is dropped by the API through the `thinking-binding-controls-2026-08-01` beta instead of failing the call with a 400), and Claude's structured outputs for the adapter's and the reviewer's response schemas, because ADK 2.11 sends no response schema to Claude: the request's `output_config.format` carries the schema made strict by the SDK's `transform_schema`, so constrained decoding enforces types, enums and required fields, and the Pydantic schema with its repairs checks the lengths and counts the grammar cannot. On `gemini` it returns `Gemini(model=..., client_kwargs={"enterprise": True, "project": ..., "location": settings.location}, retry_options=HttpRetryOptions(attempts=3, initial_delay=1))`. The bare `Gemini()` default is the retiring 2.5 Flash and is never used.
- No `FallbackModel` in phase 1. A fallback would need a second model id or the `global` endpoint, which breaks the pins; persistent 429s map to `error{code:"capacity"}`. A named fallback on `eu` is an explicit owner decision; switching `AGENT_PROVIDER` is a configuration change, not a fallback.
- Low-cardinality `labels` in every agent's generation config. On Gemini: explicit `safety_settings` at BLOCK_MEDIUM_AND_ABOVE, and no temperature, top_p or thinking budget on Gemini 3.
- `agents/anyplot/agent.py` keeps an explicit registry `ALL_AGENTS = [root_agent, *ADAPTERS.values(), reviewer]`, because the adapters and the reviewer are started with `ctx.run_node` and a tree walk from the root would miss them. A unit test iterates the registry and `App.plugins` and asserts: no `code_executor`, `google_search`, `url_context`, `AgentTool`, `LoggingPlugin` or chat-mode sub-agents; no agent has a non-empty string `instruction`; every model string and client location comes from settings.
- Settings defaults in `AgentSettings` (`agents/anyplot/settings.py`, read from `AGENT_*` variables): `provider="anthropic-vertex"`, `model="claude-haiku-5-5"`, `judge_model="claude-haiku-5-5"`, `location="eu"`, `root_thinking_budget=1024`, `adapter_thinking_budget=8192` (2,048 until 2026-10-14), `reviewer_thinking_budget=4096`, `root_effort="medium"`, `adapter_effort="high"`, `reviewer_effort="high"` (the 2026-10-11 measurement: with both, every one of the 122 cases passed the gates and 51.6 % ended `ok`, against 91.0 % and 28.7 % with thinking off, for a median of $0.0063 instead of $0.0038 per plot and a p50 of 31.5 s instead of 13.6 s; Gemini ignores the budgets and refuses a non-zero budget set explicitly, while an explicit 0 is accepted). The Gemini arm sets `AGENT_PROVIDER=gemini`, `AGENT_MODEL=gemini-3.8-flash` and `AGENT_JUDGE_MODEL=gemini-3.5-flash-lite`. The settings refuse `-latest` aliases, model ids that do not match the provider, Claude models outside `CLAUDE_MODELS` (newer Claude models refuse disabled thinking with a 400), any location other than `eu`, `us` or `global`, and `renderer="local"` outside development.

Deviations from the first sketch, each evidence-backed: there is no data-intake LLM agent in step 1 (about 320 of 325 specs declare typed `## Data` role bullets, so default bindings are deterministic and confirmed in UI dropdowns; parsing must be code anyway because `thousands='.'` silently corrupts `DD.MM.YYYY` dates); the reviewer is tool-less (GA image path, native `response_schema`, nothing persisted in session events, no `load_artifacts` listing of `data.csv`); the domain knowledge lives in tools and prompts rather than in a separate expert agent.

### Request flow for "Use with my data"

1. The `.adapt()` overlay button in `SpecDetailView` does a full navigation to `/debug/agent?spec=&library=&language=`.
2. `POST /debug/agent/sessions {spec_id, library, locale}`. The BFF validates the spec and library against `core/constants.py` and the enabled libraries from anyplot-agents `GET /v1/status` (cached), loads a catalogue snapshot through `core/catalogue/queries.py` (title, description, data roles, notes, noqa-stripped code, library version), derives the user id, and forwards it. anyplot-agents normalises the code, runs the readiness scan and the SECURITY validator, and answers `422 not_eligible` for blocked pairs (the 13 map specs, validator failures, files without a `THEME` block or with a non-literal save target). anyplot-agents has no database access in phase 1. `GET /debug/agent/eligibility?spec=&library=` gives the button the same answer. `locale` is the browser's locale, recorded with a feedback case; it sets no language, and every session starts in English (see [Session language](#session-language)).
3. `POST .../dataset {text}` runs the deterministic parse and returns a 20-row preview, the `DatasetProfile`, default bindings and warnings. A deterministic pre-filter over every header and cell for text addressed to an AI, then the dataset judge with a data rubric, refuse instruction-like datasets. Both look for injection only: names, postal and e-mail addresses, phone numbers, web addresses and amounts are the user's data and plot like any other value. The judge sees every header and the same five sample rows and top values the root and the adapter get (every value of a column with at most 12 distinct ones, else the five most frequent), with cells in full, in parts of about 4,000 characters, one judge call per part (one part for every eval fixture). When the paste carried notes around the table (`context_lines`, see [Parse](#parse)), they go through the same pre-filter and then, after the dataset judge passed the table, one scope-judge call with the scope rubric, the lines fenced as `<user_notes>`. A pre-filter hit, a verdict other than `in_scope` or no verdict drops every note with a warning and stores the table all the same; no verdict on notes is a strike, because a hidden injection in a copied web table is not the user's doing, and the judge's tokens are booked to the user like the dataset judge's (the `notes_judge` attribution line carries the verdict and the counts, never the text). The route answers the kept notes as `context_lines`, the stored dataset keeps them, and every version that `prepare_data`, `generate_data` or `.to_long()` makes from it carries them on; a new paste replaces them. Judging the notes at the paste rather than in the turn that delivers them keeps every place the root can read them (the summary turn, `get_dataset_profile`) behind one verdict, and lets the data panel show exactly what the assistant got (`# notes sent to the assistant`). No other LLM call happens for a table. Text that is no table the parser can read (prose such as "a bar chart with dog 7, cat 3 and bird 4", `label value` lines without a separator, a list of names typed one per line; at most 4,000 characters and 200 non-blank lines, so that a list without a header fits the schema's 200 rows, never a JSON array or object, which the parser reads or refuses itself) takes the transcriber path instead; free text past those limits is `422 unparseable` with the limit as its cause, never stored as it parsed. One column of typed values (integers, numbers, dates, booleans: the input of a histogram, a density or a box plot), of digit codes kept as text (`01067`) or of categories under a header, at least one repeating, is a table and never reaches the transcriber (live round 7 of 2026-10-16: 800 commute times timed out the model every time); a category list whose header has the categories' form (no `_`, brackets, `:`, `/`, `%`, `#` or digit, and no more words than a category) is stored with the short note `read '<header>' as the column name`, since no cheap rule tells `Tier` above `Hund, Katze` from `Apfel` above `Birne, Kirsche`; a header with a column name's form gets no note. A list of values without a header line keeps its first line as the first value, in a column named `value`, with a warning (`parse._header_as_row`); one of 20,001 values is refused as too many rows. A transcription whose answer stops at its output cap or breaks the schema's bounds (`models.AnswerCutOff`, not retried), or that runs out of time on a text of at least half the limits (`transcribe.near_transcribe_cap`), is `422 unparseable` with a cause that names the text's line and character count and asks for a separator; a short text that times out (a stalled endpoint: the budget is the only clock, no request has a timeout of its own), an error, a quota or an unparseable answer is `503 guard_unavailable` (owner rule of 2026-10-11): the pre-filter runs on the raw text, the judge model reads it under the `TranscribedTable` schema (`agents/anyplot/data/transcribe.py`, rubric `prompts/transcriber.md`: keep every value as written, invent and compute nothing, a note on anything ambiguous), the table's CSV is parsed like a paste, and the route answers `transcribed: true` with that CSV, the preview and the note, storing nothing. The page shows the table for the user to confirm or discard, because a wrong reading makes everything after it wrong; confirming posts the CSV through this same route with `confirmed: true`, which skips the free-text fallback (a one-column table would otherwise be read again) and the narrowing to the table (the user has seen every row, so no line is left out as a note or a total), while the deterministic parse, the pre-filter, the judge and the store run as for any paste. A binary data file reaches it the same way, through one pre-process (owner decision of 2026-10-15, part 2 of 2): Excel (`.xlsx`, `.xlsm`, `.xlsb`, `.xls`), OpenDocument (`.ods`), Parquet, Feather (v1 and v2), Arrow IPC (file and stream) and NumPy (`.npy`, `.npz`) go as base64 in JSON (the BFF's CSRF guard takes JSON only; at most 5 MB raw, the BFF refuses a larger body by its `Content-Length` before reading it) to `POST /v1/sessions/{sid}/dataset/convert`, which converts the file in the renderer's sandbox and answers the text the paste box takes; nothing is stored and no model reads anything, so the text then goes through the ordinary paste route above (its limits, pre-filter and judges) when the user presses `.parse()`, with the file's name and sheet as `source` (`Bericht.xlsx · sheet ‹2024›`; the sheet in single angle quotes since live test round 4 of 2026-10-15, so the data line's `from "…"` never nests quotes), screened as above. The renderer runs the harness in convert mode (`harness.py --convert input.bin`, `POST /convert`): like data mode it reads the bytes, loads the converter (`agents/anyplot/render/convert.py`, copied next to the harness in the image) and imports every reader it needs before it arms its guard (`ConvertGuard`: `DataGuard` with no code to run at all, but the one `compile` NumPy's `.npy` header reader makes to parse its dict literal), then parses from memory with the guard armed and writes `out.csv`, `meta.json` and `status.json`; a refusal is an exit code (`wire.CONVERT_REASONS`) with the measured value and the cap. The format comes from the first bytes, never from the name: every zip is checked from its central directory first (at most 2,000 entries, 100 MB declared uncompressed, 250 times the archive's size, no encrypted member) and every member is inflated, bounded by its declared size, to prove that size, because calamine's reader trusts none (security review of #12194); every pattern over file content is linear (the review's 30 MB run of `<c ` tags held the slot for 10 s with the first, lazy one); an OLE container with an `EncryptedPackage` stream is refused with "remove the password"; calamine (MIT) reads the values a workbook stored, never a formula, an external link, a macro or DDE; pyarrow (at least 14.0.1, CVE-2023-47248; slimmed in the image to the core, compute, IPC, Feather and Parquet, 163 MB to 99 MB, proven by converting every format inside the built image in CI) adds up the Parquet row groups (which must match the footer's total) before any page and reads at most 20,001 rows, single-threaded; NumPy loads with `allow_pickle=False` after checking the header's shape against the bytes there; pickle, joblib and HDF5 are never read. A spreadsheet goes out as its raw cell grid (`csv.writer`: dates ISO, numbers shortest, `TRUE`/`FALSE`, empty for nothing; merged cells keep the top-left value; a formula without a saved value is empty and counted for xlsx and xlsm, and a sheet of nothing but such formulas is refused with "open it in Excel and save it once"; the rows of a table block keep their empty trailing fields), so `table_block` finds a header below row 1 and keeps a title or a footnote as a note; an xlsx sheet's cell span is measured in one linear pass before calamine loads it (calamine allocates the whole bounding box: one cell at XFD1048576 was 549 GB), and any sheet is read row by row, refused past 20,200 rows or 50 columns; hidden sheets are skipped and counted, very hidden ones never named. Without a chosen sheet the first visible sheet with a cell is read and the answer names the visible sheets with cells (at most ten, whole, printable and no path; owner decision of 2026-10-15: no empty sheet in the picker); the browser keeps the file and offers a sheet picker that converts it again with the chosen sheet. Data keeps the precision it came with (owner rule of 2026-10-15): timestamps keep every fraction digit and a zone-aware one its offset in its own zone (UTC, counted, only where the zone cannot be read), decimals and 64-bit integers are written exactly, float32 in its shortest text, and the parser reads ISO timestamps with up to nine fraction digits and an offset as datetimes (`parse._as_iso_stamps`; a column with several offsets is written in UTC with a note, because pandas reads one offset per column). Parquet and Arrow structs become dotted columns, dictionaries are decoded; map, binary, union, duration and interval columns are dropped, counted and named on the page; a table with a list column goes out as JSON records instead of CSV, so the parser's own list rule (#12191: one row per value, `record_id`, thinning) expands it, with no second implementation. The text keeps the paste cap (1 MiB) and the computed-data caps (20,000 rows, 50 columns): over a cap the file is refused with its numbers, never cut; a reader that runs out of the sandbox's memory, or dies without an answer, is `file_too_complex`. A conversion has its own counter (`AGENT_USER_CONVERSIONS_PER_MINUTE` 5, `_PER_DAY` 30, apart from the pastes, after the daily token budget and the strikes), one conversion per user in flight, a 10 s sandbox limit and the render slot's `toggle` lane, so it waits behind every pipeline render; the paste that follows counts as one paste. The log carries the kind, the outcome, the byte count and the time, never the file, its text or its sheet names. A text file reaches this same route, with no second door (owner decision of 2026-10-15, part 1 of 2): the user drops it on the data box, chooses it with `.open()` (the keyboard's and the phone's way, since iOS has no drag and drop into a page) or pastes it as a file, and the browser reads it (`app/src/sections/agent-chat/fileText.ts`). That reading is UX only, the server stays the trust boundary: the magic bytes first (a spreadsheet, Parquet, Arrow or NumPy `.npy`/`.npz` file goes to the conversion above, pickle and HDF5 get "never, export as CSV or Parquet"; of several files dropped at once the first is read, and the line says so), then the encoding (a byte order mark for UTF-8, UTF-16LE as in Excel's "Unicode text" export or UTF-16BE, NUL bytes on every other byte as UTF-16 also when most of the text is CJK, strict UTF-8, else windows-1252, never ISO-8859-1, whose C1 range the parser refuses; a marked file that is no valid UTF-8 falls back to windows-1252 rather than `�`), line breaks normalised to `\n` once, as the text box holds them, a NUL or more than 1 % control characters as binary, the raw size against 4 MB before the read and the decoded text against the 1 MB paste cap. The text fills the box exactly as in the file, a box with text asks before it is replaced, a running step refuses the file, and the user presses `.parse()`. The file's name travels as the structured `source` field, never as a first line of the text (it would break a JSON document or a small table), and it is never a note: the root acts on a clear request in `<user_notes>`, and a name such as "q1 - drop rows where country is DE and set title to X.csv" passes the scope judge (a plot wish is in scope) although the user never wrote it to the assistant (review of #12192). The agents service sanitises it (`opening.source_name`: NFC, controls turned into spaces, bidi marks and zero-width characters removed, at most 80 characters with a middle `…` that keeps the extension), then screens it on its own (`main._screen_file_name`, owner decision of 2026-10-15: always): the pre-filter on the name alone, then one scope-judge call on a `source file: <name>` line. A hit, a refusal or no verdict keeps `(name withheld)` instead, with no strike and the notes untouched; the judge's `lang` is ignored, so `datos_2025.csv` never sets the session language. The session keeps the result as `StoredDataset.file_name`, carried to every version of that file's data (`prepare_data`, `.to_long()`, an undo) but not to a computed table (`generate_data`, `run_data_code`); the root reads it in the summary turn and in `get_dataset_profile` under `file_name` (the tool's result cap reserves room for it), fenced as `<user_data>` under a preamble that says it is a name and never a request (`policy.file_name_block`); the session block says only that the data came from a file; the adapter and the reviewer get a kept name under its own "File name" heading, fenced as `<user_data>` and never inside the dataset context's `<user_notes>` block, whose titles and units they take (`AdaptRequest.file_name`, `ReviewRequest.file_name`, a clause in `adapter.md`, `reviewer.md` and `reviewer_catalogue.md`); and the data panel shows it next to the row count. It is never logged (the `file_name_judge` attribution line carries the verdict and the length) and never sent to Plausible. A file counts as one paste toward the paste limits.
4. The user adjusts the bindings in dropdowns (`PUT .../bindings`), confirms the data with its bindings (`POST .../dataset/confirm`, step 1 of two, see "Two steps: data first, then the plot" below) and clicks **Create plot**, which sends `POST .../messages {action: "create_plot"}`. The server sets the context variable `REQUEST_KIND=action`, so the judge is skipped, and sends a fixed message. Since 2026-10-14 (owner decision: "queue only the pipeline, not every chat message") a turn starts at once: the stream sends `ready` and the root runs. Only the sandbox work waits in the run queue (see the bounds table below): when the root calls `plot_pipeline` (after its readiness check) or `run_data_code`, that call takes the queue's slot and the turn's start in the rate window, the stream sends `status{step:"queued", position, waiting}` while it waits, and the request deadline, paused during the wait, resumes with the time it had left when the slot comes. A plain question, a refusal, `prepare_data` and `generate_data` never wait. So a user sees `ready`, `message`, `done` for a question, and `ready`, `status{queued}` (only when another run holds the slot or the minute's start is spent), `status{adapting}` and the pipeline's steps, `plot`, `message`, `done` for a plot.
5. Root call 1 calls `plot_pipeline({})`. `PipelineArgs` has no spec, library or dataset field; those come from server-set session state, so neither the model nor an injection can redirect the pipeline. Its `theme` is the one theme the run renders and the reviewer sees; the root sets it only when the user asks for a theme. Left out, the run takes the site theme the page sent with the turn (`POST .../messages?theme=`, owner decision of 2026-11-01 after user test E2, where the dark site got a bright plot), so a change after the user switched the site follows the switch; a change of a version whose theme the user asked for keeps that theme (`CodeVersion.theme_asked`), and a caller without a page (`adk web`, the evals) keeps the latest version's theme, else light (`pipeline.run_theme`). The page sends the theme as a query parameter because the BFF's and the service's bodies are strict, and an unknown parameter lets the page, the API and the service deploy in any order. Without a dataset, with incomplete bindings or without the user's confirmation of the data the pipeline returns `PlotResult(status="not_ready", reason=...)` (`no_dataset`, `incomplete_bindings`, `data_unconfirmed`) before any LLM call.
6. The pipeline streams `adapting`, `checking`, `rendering`, `reviewing` and `repairing` and always ends with `Event(output=PlotResult)`. A `None` output would raise `NodeInterruptedError`.
7. Root call 2 writes a short reply in the session language. Since 2026-11-01 (owner decision: "short and to the point, no waffle, follow-up questions only when they really matter") a reply leads with the result in a few sentences, asks at most one question and only when the turn cannot go on without the answer or the answer would change the data or the plot materially, never closes with an offer, and acts on the user's answer to a question in the same turn; the notices the data rules require stay in every reply that needs them: the loader's "reads", "keeps time" and "converts" lines, a data change with its counts, `requested_not_found`, and what a refusal or a limit says (`prompts/root.md`, "Replies"). The version's "what changed" notes, which the adapter writes, are in the plot language and in plain words, without the code's own names such as `load_user_data()`. The UI fetches the PNG of the rendered theme (with the footer strip), `plot.py` and `data.csv` through the BFF. The light and dark switch calls `POST .../versions/{version}/render {theme}`, which renders the other theme from the version's stored run form (the same code and data) through the render backend and the host gates, with no adapter, no reviewer and no queue, and answers synchronously.
8. Free-text follow-ups go judge → root → `plot_pipeline(change_request, base="previous")`, `set_bindings`, `set_language` (a request such as "labels in English"), `keep_plot_request` (a wish for the first plot before the data is confirmed, typed or in the pasted notes, which the summary turn keeps; it starts no run, and the next `.create_plot()` draws it; the root says a request is kept only on `request_kept: true`, from this tool or from a `not_ready` `plot_pipeline`; live round 9 of 2026-10-16), or a code answer through `get_current_code`. The session's first in-scope typed message also sets the session language when the user has not chosen one. The library pills call `POST sessions/{sid}/library`, which loads a new snapshot and keeps the dataset and bindings.

The pipeline is a code-bounded loop, never a graph cycle, wrapped as `plot_pipeline = Workflow(name="plot_pipeline", input_schema=PipelineArgs, edges=[("START", run_pipeline)])` so the tool name is stable. The sketch shows the checklist flow of phase 1, which the settings still reach (`AGENT_REVIEWER_MODE=checklist`); the live defaults since 2026-10-13 are described after it:

```python
@node(rerun_on_resume=True)
async def run_pipeline(ctx: Context, node_input: PipelineArgs):
    s = PipelineState.load(ctx)                        # spec, library, snapshot, dataset_id, bindings (server-set)
    if not s.ready:
        yield Event(output=PlotResult.not_ready(s.reason)); return
    deadline = SoftDeadline(480)                       # abort_signal at 540 s is the hard backstop (140 and 180 s before 2026-10-13)
    best, best_defects, best_reviewed = None, [], False  # the best render that passed R1+R2 and whether the reviewer saw it
    review_defects, reviews, feedback, reason = [], 0, [], None
    try:
        for attempt in range(1, settings.max_attempts + 1):   # AGENT_MAX_ATTEMPTS, 2 by default
            if not budget.check(ctx): reason = "budget"; break
            if attempt > 1 and not deadline.allows(adapter_p95 + render_p95): reason = "deadline"; break   # 60 + 15 s
            yield Event(message="adapting" if attempt == 1 else "repairing")
            req = s.adapt_request(node_input, feedback, allow_full=(attempt >= 2))   # code = latest validated working form
            plan = await ctx.run_node(adapter_for(s.library), req)
            yield Event(message="checking")
            working, feedback = apply_and_validate(s, plan)   # edits → ADAPTED-profile validator (placeholder allowed)
            if feedback: continue
            yield Event(message="rendering")
            run_form = substitute_loader(normalise(working))  # the run form is the exported code
            job = s.render_job(run_form, themes=(node_input.theme,))   # one theme per run
            result = await renderer.render(job, timeout=deadline.clamp(render_timeout))
            if result.passed_host_gates:                     # R1 and R2 passed; R3 passed or was padded
                best, best_defects, best_reviewed = result, result.defects, False
            feedback = result.defects                        # R3 miss and advisory probe gates feed the next attempt while one is allowed
            if feedback and attempt < settings.max_attempts: continue
            if reviews == 2: break                           # both reviews used: nothing left to repair for
            yield Event(message="reviewing")
            verdict = await ctx.run_node(reviewer, s.review_request(run_form, result))
            reviews, best_reviewed = reviews + 1, True
            review_defects = [d.as_line() for d in verdict.defects]
            feedback = review_defects
            if verdict.ok: break
    except Exception:
        reason = "error"
    finally:
        yield Event(output=s.finish(ctx, best, best_defects, best_reviewed, review_defects, reason))  # every path
```

`finish` returns `ok` only when `best` exists, the reviewer saw exactly that render (`best_reviewed`) and passed it, and the canvas was not padded; `needs_attention` when `best` exists but either the last review rejected it (its lines become residual defects), or the reviewer never saw it after giving defects (a repair whose second review the budget or the deadline skipped, or whose answer could not be read; the first review's lines become residual defects), or the canvas had to be padded, or advisory probe gates still report defects; `failed` when no render passed R1 and R2, with `reason` in `validation`, `render`, `deadline`, `budget` or `error`. The translator maps an abort without a `PlotResult` to `error{code:"deadline"}`.

Since 2026-10-13 the defaults change the loop in four places (the measurements are in `agents/README.md`, "The plot pipeline since 2026-10-13"):

- The reviewer scores each render with the catalogue rubric (`AGENT_REVIEWER_MODE=catalogue`), and the code decides from the points: a round below 95 % of the technical points (`AGENT_REVIEW_BAR`, 1.0 until 2026-10-14) goes to the repair, and so does a round whose review names a visible defect, text cut at the canvas edge (AR-09), unreadable text (VQ-01), overlap (VQ-02) or a cut or crammed layout (VQ-05), whatever its points (`pipeline.VISIBLE_DEFECT_IDS`, since live test round 7 of 2026-10-16: two lost points had let a heatmap with a clipped axis label ship after the first try; VQ-01 by owner decision of the same day). Such a render is not clean for ship-best, so a clean repair a few points lower ships over it, and a render that ships with such lines carries them as notes at any score; a shipped render at or above 95 % of them (`AGENT_STATUS_BAR`) with no measured finding left shows without notes. A review that cannot back its score (a technical criterion left unscored, or points deducted without a defect line) is asked once more, like an answer that fails its schema.
- Every round is reviewed, and the best-scored render ships (`AGENT_SHIP_BEST`).
- Before the first review the adapter proofreads its own render against the rubric the reviewer scores with, the render's measured findings included (`AGENT_SELF_REVIEW`); a repair round goes to the review without a proofread since 2026-10-14 (`AGENT_REPAIR_SELF_REVIEW`). The proofread is part of the round, never a round of its own.
- An adapter answer that fails its check gets one more call in the same round (`AGENT_CHECK_RETRY`). Since 2026-10-16 (owner decision, live case "heat3" of round 8, where a full-file repair changed the theme statements twice and the run shipped its first render), an answer whose only failure is a change to the protected `THEME` assignment, theme tokens or final save statement, after the round's retry is spent, gets those statements restored from the code before the plan (`edits.restore_protected`; never when a theme name is bound again) and goes on through the check, the render and the review; the attribution line `pipeline_check` logs it as `drift_restored`.
- Since 2026-10-14 the adapter's instruction holds the reviewer's rubric verbatim (`scoring.rubric_text`), not only its one-line criteria rows; measured findings (advisory gate lines, ADAPTATION findings) go to the self-review and the review first and start a repair only when they are still in the reviewed render (`AGENT_GATE_REPAIR=review`); and they stay in the notes of the shipped render whatever its score, so `ok` never hides a measured defect.

Since 2026-10-14 two more rules hold (the owner's live test of that day):

- `PlotResult.changes` lists the notes of every adapter call up to the shipped render whose plan changed the code. A proofread that changed nothing adds nothing, so its "already present" notes never replace the real changes, and a shipped earlier round reports its own.
- What the user asked for wins over the rubric. Each version keeps the change requests it carries out (`CodeVersion.requests`); a change of it passes them to every adapter call and to the reviewer as `user_requests`, fenced as user text, and the prompts keep them as the user's standing decisions: never a defect, changed only when the current change request asks for it.

Since 2026-10-15 the root's replies are grounded in what the server measured, not in what a model meant to do (the live tests of 2026-10-14/15: a reply claimed "about 6,5 Mio." on an axis that drew "6.0 M", another said the title was not set while the image showed one, and two summaries described other bindings than the real ones):

- The probe reports the content of the drawn texts by role (`labels`: titles, axis labels and legend texts first, then up to 100 tick labels per axis of each axes, each tick and axis label with the index of its axes, colorbar ticks under their own kind, texts wholly off the canvas left out, at most 400 texts of 200 characters and 20,000 characters together, and `labels_truncated` when any drawn text was left out or cut, in which case no requested text is claimed missing; `harness.drawn_labels`). `grounding.plot_texts` groups them into `PlotShows.texts`, clipped for the reply, and the shipped result carries `shows` (those texts, the review's verdict and score, and `requested_not_found`). The root reads it in its own `<tool_notes>` block next to the adapter's `changes`, and the prompt says to describe the plot from `shows` and never to state a value, format, binding or text that no tool result holds. `shows` never reaches the page's `plot` event. Since 2026-11-01 the libraries that draw without matplotlib report their drawn texts too (user test of 2026-11-01: Chart.js, bokeh and pygal replies said the plot's title could not be read back, and a Makie reply offered a title the plot already had): pygal from the SVG cairosvg converts, altair from vl-convert's scenegraph, and lets-plot from its bridge's SVG, each by the library's own roles (`harness.install_text_probes`); Chart.js, D3, ECharts, Highcharts, MUI X, plotly and bokeh from the page the browser harness screenshotted (`render.mjs`, `writeTextProbe`, sandbox mode only): SVG and HTML texts by the library's classes (an inverted Highcharts chart's axes by the side they are drawn on), D3's ticks by their axis's `text-anchor` and the one rotated text in the outer tenth as its y axis label, the one largest text in the top fifth as the title of a library that names none, ECharts' texts from zrender's display list by the view that owns them (an axis name or tick by the axis view's own mark), and Chart.js's and bokeh's from their chart models, which a plugin's own drawing is not in, so those two probes report themselves incomplete. ggplot2 and Makie report them through a text probe their rows load (`text_probe.R` as R's user profile, which wraps `ggsave` and, after a save that returned, reads the saved plot's grob tree by layout cell, so its second build never reaches the PNG; `text_probe.jl` with `-L`, whose `atexit` hook reads the current figure's blocks, rich text as drawn): each leaves `texts-<theme>.json`, which the harness's exec mode turns into the probe under the same caps (`harness.collect_text_probe`). A subtitle has a role of its own (`subtitle`), so it is never trimmed with the other texts. The probes are best-effort: one that fails, or a page that does not answer within 2 s, drops the labels and writes `{"error": <class>}` instead, which the `pipeline_render` line logs as `probe_error` (a closed vocabulary, `grounding.PROBE_ERRORS`), and never fails or changes the render; on the catalogue's bar-grouped they add 0 to 0.11 s per render (median of five, local Docker backend), ggplot2 about 0.3 s (the plot's grob built a second time) and Makie about 0.55 s (the hook's code compiled at exit) (`RuntimeAdapter.text_probe` is True for every library). When a render reports no texts, the root gets `shows` without a `texts` key (`tool_safety.fence_pipeline_notes`) and quotes, names or asks about no text of the plot; since `texts` can leave out a text the plot draws, the root never says the plot lacks one either, and only `requested_not_found` says a requested text is missing. The eval harness counts the shipped runs without texts of the libraries whose render reports them (`untexted`, from the `texts` and `texts_expected` fields of the `pipeline_result` line) and the passages the root's replies to a shipped plot quote that no drawn or requested text holds (`unbacked_quotes`, compared in process, only the count kept).
- `PipelineArgs.expected_texts` lists the texts and number formats the user wrote out for the plot, verbatim (at most five). The adapter gets them fenced as user text; `grounding.expected_misses` checks each against all drawn texts, never the clipped summary. A text, numbers in it included (`Population 2025`, `Top 10`), must stand whole in one drawn text, case, spacing, quotes, dashes and mathtext aside. A number-format example (a number with one short unit: `6,5 Mio.`, `12 %`, `CHF 6,5`) must draw its unit, and every text it applies to (the texts that draw the unit, and the tick labels of that axis of that axes, or of that colorbar, whose label or ticks draw it) must use its decimal mark, dates and three-digit groups aside. A probe without `labels` (a renderer before this change, or a failed text report) checks nothing and adds the residual line "the requested texts could not be checked" instead of a silent pass. A miss is a measured finding under `AGENT_EXPECT_CHECK=repair` (the default): the self-review and the review see it, a round whose render still misses it is repaired with it, and a shipped render that misses it carries an `SC-02` or `SC-04` residual line.
- A render on a runtime whose reviewer checks the requested texts (`RuntimeAdapter.review_texts`, owner decision of 2026-10-29) hands them to its review instead, in the same reviewer call. Today that is R, Julia, JavaScript, and altair, pygal, lets-plot, plotly and bokeh among the Python libraries: their probes report no texts or read them for the reply alone, since a chart model is not every drawn text, so they never claim a requested text missing. Before, every ggplot2 request for a title or an axis label shipped as `needs_attention` with the "could not be checked" line, although the image showed the text. Only such a render's review request carries the texts, under a fixed heading and fenced as user text, so the reviewer prompt and the review request of a render with the probe are unchanged. The reviewer looks for each text in the image and reports one it cannot find as a defect line that quotes it, under `SC-04`, or `SC-02` for a number format in the catalogue rubric (the checklist has only `SC-04`). `grounding.reviewed_misses` reads those lines back from every line of the answer, whatever the points: a line names a requested text when a quoted passage of it reads as that text. A reported miss is handled like the probe's: under `AGENT_EXPECT_CHECK=repair` it sends the round to the repair whatever the points, as a visible defect does, the render is not clean for `AGENT_SHIP_BEST`, and a shipped render that misses it carries the reviewer's line as a residual line and the text in `requested_not_found`. A text no line names counts as found. The "could not be checked" line stays only on a render whose review did not run or could not be read. matplotlib, seaborn and plotnine keep the old path, because they draw through matplotlib's probe (`PythonRuntime.review_texts` by library, `REVIEWED_TEXT_LIBRARIES`).
- A shipped result reaches the root with two server facts beside the notes: `data_confirmed: true` and, since live test round 7 of 2026-10-16, `data_changed: false` (`tool_safety.fence_pipeline_notes`): the pipeline never changes the data. The root prompt says to claim a row, category or value added, removed, filled in or corrected only when a data tool's `changes` in the same turn says so, and to describe a plot note about an "Other" group or a remainder as part of the plot; the adapter prompt says its `changes` describe what the code does with this data, never a branch that does not run for it. The case: a donut of six energy sources that already summed to 100 % got the reply "I added 'Übrige' at 2.0 % so the shares reach 100 %", although that row was the user's. No reply-side check parses the claim: in any language a deterministic one would be a word list.
- `get_dataset_profile` returns the bindings in force (`bindings`, one `role: column` per line inside a `<user_data>` fence), the session block's bindings line points to it, and the root prompt says to name exactly those in the summary. The list stands in the tool result, not in the session block: in a measurement with the list in the block, the root took it for text the user had written in 1 of 6 summaries.
- The adapter and the reviewer get a dataset context block, fenced as `<user_notes>` and capped at 1,500 characters (`dataset_context.py`): the notes pasted with the data, which the dataset route kept only after the pre-filter and the scope judge (a JSON-stat title and source among them), and where a computed table comes from (`StoredDataset.source`: the ranges and expressions of `generate_data`, or the function, call and variable names of a `run_data_code` snippet, never its comments or strings). A change of the data keeps the source, and a new paste drops it.

#### Bounds

The owner decided the queue, the rate and the serial renders on 2026-10-09, after spikes S and S2 showed that one instance with 4 GiB serves one sandbox at a time safely. On 2026-10-14 he narrowed the queue to the sandbox work, after a live test in which plain answers and even scope refusals waited about 60 s each for the rate window ("bei sowas muss schnell die antwort kommen"), and added the per-user message limits and the session close that bound chat turns instead.

| Bound | Value | Enforced by |
|---|---|---|
| Adapter calls per run | 2 rounds (`AGENT_MAX_ATTEMPTS`; an eval may set 1 or 3), each with at most one check retry (`AGENT_CHECK_RETRY`) and one self-review | the pipeline loop |
| Reviewer calls per run | One per round, `AGENT_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (2) at most, with `AGENT_SHIP_BEST`; 2 (`MAX_REVIEWS`) without it | the pipeline loop |
| Self-review calls per run | One adapter call in the first round (`AGENT_SELF_REVIEW`; every round with `AGENT_REPAIR_SELF_REVIEW`): the adapter proofreads its render before the review; not a repair and not a round | the pipeline loop |
| Renders per run | one round per attempt, of one theme (`PipelineArgs.theme`, `light` by default) | the pipeline loop |
| `plot_pipeline` calls per invocation | 1 | `ToolSafety` |
| LLM calls per request | 16 since 2026-10-13 (12 before): `RunConfig(max_llm_calls=16, streaming_mode=StreamingMode.NONE)`. `ADK_MAX_LLM_CALLS=20` only sets the default for runs without a `RunConfig`, such as `adk web` and evals | the budget plugin and the `RunConfig` |
| Render time | 60 s per render, host-enforced and clamped to the remaining soft deadline | the pipeline's clamp and the renderer's kill path (`timeout_s` per theme) |
| Request deadline | 540 s since 2026-10-13 (180 s before) through `abort_signal` plus task cancellation on disconnect, counted from the start of the turn; paused while a sandbox run waits in the queue and resumed with the time it had left when the run leaves the queue, so a turn's active time stays within 540 s and the whole turn within 540 s plus the queue's 600 s maximum wait, below the BFF's 1,190 s turn cap | the `/messages` route (`RunDeadline`) |
| What queues | since 2026-10-14 only sandbox work: a turn's first `plot_pipeline` call that passed its readiness check, or its first `run_data_code` call, takes the queue entry and holds it until the turn ends; a second sandbox run in the same turn neither waits nor books a second start. A chat turn without one (an answer, a refusal, `prepare_data`, `generate_data`, a pipeline call that answers `not_ready`) never enters the queue | the `/messages` route (`TurnSlot`) |
| Runs in flight per instance | 1 (`AGENT_RUN_CONCURRENCY`) sandbox run | the run queue |
| Run starts | fewer than `AGENT_RUNS_PER_MINUTE` (1) in the last 60 s, a sliding window of start times; one start per turn that runs a sandbox | the run queue |
| Wait in the queue | at most `AGENT_QUEUE_MAX_WAIT_S` (600 s), mid-turn; a run that waited that long ends the turn with `error{code:"capacity"}`, unless its turn comes at that very moment. Through the BFF, a whole turn ends by `AGENT_TURN_MAX_S` (1,190 s since 2026-10-13, 890 s before), below anyplot-api's 1,200 s request timeout, so the root's first call, the full wait and the 540 s run fit | the run queue; `AGENT_TURN_MAX_S` in the BFF |
| Queue length | rate x maximum wait / 60, so 10 entries at the defaults; a sandbox run past that ends its turn with `error{code:"capacity"}` and the run is aborted before the pipeline's first model call. The formula assumes runs within the 60 s rate window, so admission promises a place, not a start | the run queue |
| Runs per session | one turn in flight (answering, queued or running), one theme toggle or one paste; anything more in the same session gets `409 run_active`. Since 2026-10-21 (owner decision) the exclusion is per session only: each browser window opens a session of its own, and a user's other sessions never block it | the run registry (`Runtime.active`, `Runtime.pasting`) |
| Sandbox runs per user | at most `AGENT_USER_MAX_RUNS` (3) queued or running at the same time across the user's sessions, since 2026-10-21; the turn whose run would be one more ends with `error{code:"run_limit"}` before the queue's length is checked, and the run is aborted before the pipeline's first model call | the run queue (`RunQueue.submit`, `QueueUserLimit`) |
| Messages per user | at most `AGENT_USER_MESSAGES_PER_MINUTE` (10) chat turns in any 60 s and `AGENT_USER_MESSAGES_PER_DAY` (200) per UTC day, typed messages and actions alike, counted in the usage book; one more gets the fixed `rate_limited` or `daily_limit` refusal right after `ready`, before the scope judge, so a flood costs no model call. A refused turn is not counted | the `/messages` route (`UsageBook.message_limit`) |
| Pastes per user | at most `AGENT_USER_PASTES_PER_MINUTE` (10) in any 60 s and `AGENT_USER_PASTES_PER_DAY` (100) per UTC day (owner decision of 2026-10-14), across sessions, checked before any parser or model; only a paste that reaches the pre-filter or a judge counts (a formula, code or unparseable text is refused for free). One more is `429 rate_limited` or `429 daily_limit`. One paste per session at a time, and none while a turn or a theme toggle of the session runs (`409 run_active`); since 2026-10-21 the user's other sessions paste in parallel, and the counts stay per user; a closed session takes none (`403 session_closed`). Since 2026-10-14: a paste spends dataset-judge calls, a notes-judge call and maybe a transcriber call, and had only the daily token check | the `/dataset` route (`UsageBook.paste_limit`, `Runtime.pasting`) |
| File conversions per user | at most `AGENT_USER_CONVERSIONS_PER_MINUTE` (5) in any 60 s and `AGENT_USER_CONVERSIONS_PER_DAY` (30) per UTC day (owner decision of 2026-10-15), across sessions, counted when the file goes to the sandbox and checked after the daily token budget and the strikes; the paste that follows counts as one paste. One conversion per session at a time (`409 run_active`; per user before 2026-10-21), at most 5 MB, 10 s in the sandbox, behind every pipeline render in the render slot (`503 capacity` after 20 s) | the `/dataset/convert` route (`UsageBook.convert_limit`, `Runtime.converting`) |
| Scope refusals per session | the third counted refusal of a session (`AGENT_SESSION_REFUSALS`: `out_of_scope` or `attack`; `needs_data` never counts, `other_library` only with `AGENT_OTHER_LIBRARY_COUNTS`) closes it: that refusal and every later turn of the session answer `session_closed` ("reload the page to start a new session"), the later ones before the judge. The counted refusal before the closing one carries the fixed `close_warning` sentence (`AGENT_SESSION_CLOSE_WARNING`), after a live test of 2026-10-14 closed a session unwarned. Counted from the session's withheld messages, so the count ends with the session; a closed session's data is not carried into the next one (follow-up). The per-user strikes (`AGENT_ATTACK_STRIKES`, 3 `attack` verdicts per day across sessions and the dataset judge) end in the `locked_out` refusal for the day (the `budget` text before 2026-10-14, which read as a spent usage limit); the strikes live in the instance's memory and reset at midnight UTC or on a restart (a redeploy or a scale-to-zero) | the ScopeGuard, the `/messages` and `/dataset` routes |
| Daily budget | a user already over the daily token budget gets the `budget` refusal at once, before the judge | the `/messages` route |
| Concurrent renders per instance | 1 (`AGENT_RENDER_CONCURRENCY`), one theme per slot, for every backend and every route; a waiting pipeline render gets a freed slot before a waiting theme toggle | `SerialRenderer` (`render/serial.py`), applied in `Services.backend` |
| Theme toggle | one render; it waits for a render slot at most `AGENT_REQUEST_DEADLINE_S` - `AGENT_RENDER_TIMEOUT_S` (120 s), then answers `503 capacity`; a theme that failed the host gates twice is answered from its record | `theme_render.py` |
| Dataset size | at most 1,048,576 bytes of paste (204,800 until 2026-10-14; computed data 2,097,152), 20,000 rows, 50 columns and 200 characters per cell; one past any of them is refused before any model call (`413 too_long` for the bytes, `422 unparseable` for the rest). The canonical `data.csv` can grow to about 3.8 times the input (`7e15` becomes `7000000000000000.0`); the parser refuses a parse whose canonical CSV passes `MAX_CANONICAL_BYTES` (4 MiB, the renderer's `data_csv` limit, 2 MiB before), so the 1 MB `stress-csv-expansion` case (3,954,696 bytes canonical) still fits. Measured on a 914,795-byte paste of 19,999 rows: the parse 0.5 s, the pre-filter over every cell 0.22 s, one dataset-judge part (the judge reads headers and a sample, never every row). The dataset store accounts each version at its canonical CSV (up to 4 MiB) and profile, and a session keeps its current version and five undo versions, about 25 MiB at worst, so its cap rose from 64 MiB to 256 MiB (about ten such sessions per instance, inside the service's 4 GiB) | the `/dataset` route and `parse.py` (stress cases `stress-cap-*`, `stress-over-*`, `stress-csv-expansion`) |
| Dataset profile in the adapter request | at most `MAX_ADAPTER_PROFILE_BYTES` (16 KiB of UTF-8, measured like the root's `get_dataset_profile` limit on the profile inside its `<user_data>` fence, so neither CJK or emoji cells nor tag text that the fence escapes can stretch it): the sample rows go first, then the top values past the five most frequent, then the rest of the top values, then the ranges and statistics, and every column stays. 47 text columns of long values make the full profile 26,911 characters (29,491 bytes; the model-facing JSON carries no null field) | `briefs.adapter_profile` in `render_adapt_request` (stress case `stress-cap-profile`) |
| Plotted points | scatter marks plus line vertices over `MAX_PLOTTED_POINTS` (500,000 since 2026-10-14, the owner's bound after the paste and computed-data caps rose: 20,000 rows of 24 series pass, the stress cases' 610,000 and 1,000,000 computed points still trip it; 200,000 before) are G9, an advisory DQ-03 line for the repair. Measured on WSL2: a Lorenz attractor of 1,000,000 steps renders in 2.0 s of CPU (2.1 s wall) at a 479 MiB peak and still renders with the address space cut to 610 MiB, so G9 is its bound. At 10,000,000 steps it peaks at 889 MiB and fails from 1,015 MiB down, so under the 1 GiB address space only a few MiB of platform overhead decide between G9 and R1. A 50,000-squared distance matrix fails at once under the 1 GiB address space (R1) | the harness probe and `gates.py` (stress cases `stress-compute-*`) |
| Probe texts | the probe measures the first 2,000 drawn texts (`PROBE_MEASURE_LIMIT`), because each extent costs CPU time under the sandbox's limit, and keeps at most 400 text boxes per render, the ones past a canvas edge first, so G3 sees every clipped text among them; `texts_total` counts every drawn text | `harness.py` |
| Missing glyphs | the probe lists at most 16 code points without a glyph (`GLYPH_SAMPLE_LIMIT`); G10 names at most three, each label at most 26 characters, and accepts a count of at most 0x110000, so its line fits the reviewer's 300-character note even for a forged probe | `harness.py` and `render/gates.py` |

The stress cases (`agents/evals/fixtures/cases/stress-*`) each name the bound that must stop them; `python -m agents.evals.matrix --cases stress --static` checks the model-free ones without a model, and the [fixtures README](../../agents/evals/fixtures/README.md#stress-cases-what-bounds-them) records what each measured.

A typical "Create plot" costs 4 LLM calls (root twice, adapter, reviewer); a repair adds one, and the review of a render repaired after a rejection one more; each free-text turn adds one judge call; the theme toggle costs none.

The run queue (`agents/anyplot/run_queue.py`) is an in-process FIFO in front of the sandbox runs, the memory, rate and cost limiter of the instance. Since 2026-10-14 a turn takes its entry mid-turn: the `/messages` route runs the ADK invocation in a task of its own and gives the request ledger `run_slot` (`TurnSlot.take` in `agents/main.py`), which `plot_pipeline` and `run_data_code` await before they render or run code; the queue positions it reports go through the stream's outbox while the tool is blocked. It has two lanes: a `premium` entry goes before every `normal` one (first come, first served within a lane), but the concurrency and rate limits bind it too. Nothing sets `premium` yet; it is the lane for users who later pay for their own tokens. While a run waits, the stream sends `status{step:"queued", position, waiting}` at once, on every change, and every 15 s unchanged, so idle proxies keep the stream open; `position` 1 runs next and `waiting` counts every queued entry, this one included. The BFF restarts its turn budget on each of them and on the first event after the wait, as before, because it never assumed the wait comes first. A client that disconnects while it waits leaves the queue, and `POST .../cancel` takes a waiting run out at once and ends the turn. A full queue or an expired wait fails the turn with `capacity` and aborts it, so the root spends no closing call and the page sees the same `error{code:"capacity"}` as before.
