Routes mirror `/v1` plus `GET eligibility`, `POST sessions/{sid}/feedback`, and the triage routes `GET /debug/agent/cases`, `GET /debug/agent/cases/{id}` and `PATCH /debug/agent/cases/{id}`. The deploy smoke test expects 401 on `/debug/agent/status`.
- **SSE protocol `anyplot/1`**, translated from ADK events and never forwarded raw: `ready{v, run_id}`, sent when the turn starts, at once; `status{step:"queued", position, waiting}` mid-turn while its `plot_pipeline` or `run_data_code` call waits in the run queue, at once, on every change and every 15 s unchanged (`position` 1 runs next, `waiting` counts every queued entry, this one included); `status{step, attempt}` for the pipeline's steps; `message{text}` (root final text only), `plot{PlotResult}`, `refusal{code, text}`, `error{code, ref}` with codes `capacity` (also for a run that waited the queue's maximum), `run_limit` (since 2026-10-21, a sandbox run past the user's `AGENT_USER_MAX_RUNS` across sessions), `deadline`, `guard_unavailable`, `upstream` and `internal`, and `done{llm_calls, tokens, bindings?, data_confirmed?, dataset?, language?}` (the last four are optional: after a run `bindings` and `data_confirmed` carry the session's bindings and whether the data is confirmed, so the page syncs its dropdowns and its step after a turn in which the root's `set_bindings` changed them, `dataset` carries the new data version after a turn in which the root's `prepare_data` or `generate_data` changed the data, and `language` carries the session language as `{language, source}`, `source` one of `default`, `pasted`, `detected` and `chosen`, so the page syncs its chip (the dataset route's answer carries the same field after a paste); a turn refused before the queue, such as the budget refusal, and a session gone meanwhile omit them). The translator tolerates the ADK 2.x `node_info` and `output` fields. A user already over the daily budget gets `ready`, `refusal{code:"budget"}` and `done` without waiting in the queue. Because queued time does not count toward the agents service's deadline, the BFF restarts its own turn budget (`AGENT_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_S`) on every queued status, with the queue's 15 s heartbeat on top because the run may start that long before its first event, and on the first event after the wait. Nothing moves a turn past `AGENT_TURN_MAX_S` (1,190 s), which stays below anyplot-api's `--timeout=1200`: a turn still queued when its run could no longer finish inside that cap ends with `error{code:"capacity"}`, and closing the upstream stream takes it out of the queue before it spends a token.
- **Frontend.** A lazy `app/src/pages/AgentChatPage.tsx` at `debug/agent`, laid out as one chat column (since 2026-10-11, "chat first"). The data card (`app/src/sections/agent-chat/DataPanel.tsx`) is the first turn of the thread: a textarea with a 1 MB counter (200 KB until 2026-10-14), preview table, binding dropdowns, `.confirm()` at its foot, and the `# changed` block with `.undo()` after the assistant changed the data or computed it from a formula, without `.undo()` when the formula made the session's first data; a formula pasted into the textarea gets the server's `422 formula`, and the card offers `.send()`, which sends the text to the chat as a turn. The assistant's summary of a stored parse is the next turn, and each data change updates the card in place. After `.confirm()` the card collapses into a header pinned under the site's sticky masthead (`✓ data fits · N rows · M columns · version V`), whose `.show()` opens the full card again without leaving step 2; any binding change, undo, data request or new paste drops the confirmation on the server, and the page returns to step 1 with the card open. The header then stays, as `! confirm again · N rows · M columns · version V` with the phrase in bold on a warning-tinted surface, and its `.show()` leads back to the card from anywhere in the thread, for example after a data request sent from below the plot. The thread gets a warning notice that names what dropped the confirmation (`bindings changed`, `the data changed`, `not confirmed on the server`), and the card's step line repeats it next to `.confirm()`; the first binding setup after a paste, where nothing was confirmed yet, gets none. The step line follows the data the card shows: a data request or undo after the loss names the changed data, and a new paste, which nobody has confirmed yet, names no loss. A table read from free text that waits for `.use()` or `.discard()` holds the page in step 1, even when a turn reports the server's confirmation of the stored data. While the header shows, the page's `scroll-padding-top` keeps keyboard focus and scrolled-to content below it. Below the thread sit a progress grid (since 2026-10-12, the owner's variant A: one row per round, `first try`, `second try`, ... (`repair 1` until live round 9 of 2026-10-16, which read as no second round; a running repair round says what it repairs in words on the right, `repairing text size, layout`, from the rubric ids, `Round.defects`, that the round before passed on), the same four steps in the same columns so each step lines up under the round above, and how the round ended on the right, such as `✓ ok`, `✗ review: 3 findings`, `✗ 2 measured`, `✗ code check failed` or, for the shipped round of a `needs_attention` result, `~ 1 finding open` (the round marked `shipped`, which is not always the last: a repair that fails its check leaves the earlier render shipped); the step a round stopped at shows a red ✗ and the steps after it a hollow dot; the facts come from `PlotResult.rounds` and the `outcome` and `findings` that the `repairing` status carries for the round before it; the result card keeps the same grid under `# rounds` and names the shipped round the same way (`second try`), or, when an earlier round's render won, which one (`first try shipped of 2`); on a phone the round's label and outcome take a line above its steps), a Stop button (`AbortController` plus the cancel route) and the composer. While a turn without pipeline steps runs (the summary, a question, a data request), the thread shows an in-progress line in the assistant's slot (`summarizing your data …`, `working on your message …`) until the first reply or pipeline step; the progress grid shows only for a plot run, a queue position or pipeline steps, so the two never say the same thing. While anything is in flight, the bindings block says that the bindings and `.confirm()` wait, and a running parse says so under `.parse()`. Once a dataset exists, the composer always shows `.create_plot()`: before the confirmation as the quiet button next to `.send()`, whose click calls nothing and shows a notice under the composer that leads to `.confirm()` (`# confirm the data first · …`, with `.show()`, which scrolls to the button and focuses it; for a table read from text that still waits, it leads to `.use()`). The notice reads the data card at every render, so a table read from text that arrives or is discarded after the click changes its text and the target of its `.show()`. In step 2 it is the filled action: with an empty box it starts the first plot as it is, with text the text goes as the user's turn, so the assistant makes the first plot with that change. Once the library has a plot, the composer refines with `.send()` (a question or a change on the latest version) and keeps a quiet `.start_over()` beside it (since 2026-10-12, `.create_plot()` before: the owner could not tell the two apart), described as "new from the catalogue plot, without your changes": a fresh plot of the same data from the catalogue code. A wide table (since 2026-10-12, `agents/anyplot/data/shape.py`): when the spec wants one required label role and exactly one required numeric role, no numeric series family, and the table holds 2 to 20 numeric columns and no text or boolean column (date columns are kept as keys), the dataset answers carry `suggestion`, the `pivot_longer` into two columns named in the session language (`Weekday`, `Month` or `Year` when every melted header is a weekday, month or year of one language, else `Category`, and `Value`; German `Wochentag`, `Monat`, `Jahr`, `Kategorie`, `Wert`; `shape.LONG_NAMES` for en, de, fr, es, it, pt, nl and pl, English otherwise; English for every session until live round 9 of 2026-10-16, where `Weekday` and `Value` became a German heatmap's axis and colorbar titles; the language route and every turn's `done` return the recomputed offer, so the card always names what `.to_long()` applies; a name that an input column already has gives way to the role's name, then to a numbered one, so names never decide the offer; `.to_long()` binds the two new columns to the roles they were made for, `LongSuggestion.bindings`, so `Category` never takes a spec's `category` role by its name), since live test round 8 of 2026-10-16, where the role names `y` and `value` became the plot's title "Wert nach Wochentag und Stunde"; the data card shows it as `# wide table` with `.to_long()`, which `POST /v1/sessions/{sid}/dataset/to_long` applies without a model call as the next version with the new columns bound to their roles, the user's bindings on the kept columns carried over and the defaults for the rest (undo as usual), and the root's profile tool carries it as `to_long` so the summary names it in one sentence. Since live test round 7 of 2026-10-16 a wide matrix gets it too: a spec whose required roles are two label roles of a grid or grouped chart (`shape.MATRIX_ROLES`: a heatmap's `x` and `y`, a grouped box's `category` and `subcategory`, a stacked bar's `component`; never a flow's `source` and `target`, a hierarchy's `name` and `parent` or a party's `color`) and one numeric role, and a table of one row-label column with a distinct label in every row (the one text column, else the one date column, else a first column of whole numbers that is a year or every hour of a range) plus at least three numeric columns (`Stunde;Mo;...;So`) is offered the `pivot_longer` of the numeric columns into the second label role, the row labels kept as the key for the first. Years and parts of a date are keys in both rules, never melted (an unnamed 1-to-12 column counts as a month only right after a year column that repeats), and so is a first column that numbers the rows (`id`, `Nr`, `Stunde` 0 to 3), which keeps the category of `id,a,b,c` on `id`. The numeric columns the offer melts (`bindings.label_siblings`) are never bound by default to a role that takes labels, also when `carry_bindings` refills the open roles after an undo: the role stays open, so the bindings are incomplete until `.to_long()` (the default had bound the heatmap's `y` to `Mo` and `value` to `Di`, and a confirmed run drew a meaningless matrix after 170 s). Bound so by hand, the binding is valid with a warning, shown under the dropdown (the dataset answer's `suggestion.siblings`) and in the session block, where the root offers `.to_long()` before any plot; blocking `.confirm()` on it is an open owner decision. The chat header carries a language chip next to the library pills (since 2026-10-12): `lang: en`, `lang: de · from data` after a paste set it from the notes around the table or the column names (since 2026-10-14; one `#` notice), `lang: de · detected` after the first typed message set it (the thread then shows one `#` line that names the chip), or `lang: de · chosen`. A click opens a popover with the ten common languages and a field for any other language in Latin letters; a choice calls the language route, and a `done` with `language` updates the chip, never moving it from `chosen` back down to `detected` or `default`. Keyboard focus follows the steps: after the user's own `.confirm()` it moves to `.create_plot()` before the first plot and to the header's `.show()` after it, a turn that reports the confirmation again leaves it where it is, and a key press on a composer button that a run replaces with Stop moves it into the message box, so a second press never stops the run. The **result card** (`app/src/sections/agent-chat/ResultCard.tsx`) reuses the plot page's overlay actions: the rendered plot shown inline as a blob URL, in a box that takes the loaded PNG's own aspect because the footer strip makes it taller than 16:9, with a light and dark switch that renders the other theme on demand through the theme toggle route, **Copy image** (Clipboard API `navigator.clipboard.write([new ClipboardItem({"image/png": blob})])`, falling back to download where unsupported), **Download PNG** for every rendered theme and **Open full size**; the adapted code in `CodeHighlighter` with **Copy code** (`useCopyCode`, one click), **Download plot.py** and **Download data.csv** (the pair runs unchanged); the change list and residual notes; and the quick-feedback control. The card has no composer of its own (since 2026-10-12): a change request or a question about the plot goes through the thread's one composer below it, and the assistant decides whether it is a change or an answer. Earlier versions stay reachable in the thread, each with its own image and code. The same card is reused unchanged when the feature goes public. `app/src/lib/sse.ts` parses the stream over `fetchWithAuth(...).body.getReader()`. The `.adapt()` button is the fourth overlay button in `app/src/sections/spec-detail/SpecDetailView.tsx`, wired through `onUseWithMyData` from `SpecPage.tsx`, and renders only when `CONFIG.features.agentChat && (CONFIG.isDev || adminHint) && eligible`, where `adminHint` is a localStorage flag (`anyplot.adminHint`, `app/src/utils/adminAuth.ts`) that `DebugPage` sets after `/debug/status` succeeds and clears on a refusal, and `eligible` comes from the eligibility route (`app/src/hooks/useAgentEligibility.ts`). It does a full navigation so Cloudflare Access can intercept, and public pages never probe `/api/debug/*`. As built on 2026-10-10: the dataset response lists the spec's roles (`{name, kinds, required, variadic, description}`), so every role gets a dropdown, a variadic family one per member (`y1`, `y2`, ...) plus an empty one for the next, kept contiguous on the client; a refused binding set answers with the check's lines, which the panel lists; the `plot` event carries the stored `version`, which addresses the artifact and theme toggle routes (see [Agent chat](../reference/api.md#agent-chat-admin-only)); the page starts nothing while a turn, a theme render, a parse or a binding change is in flight, since the server would refuse it with `409 run_active`; Stop calls the cancel route and keeps the stream open until the server's `done` (at most 30 s), so a result stored just before the stop still arrives; the session is also deleted on `pagehide` with `keepalive`, and since nothing restores it, the page asks before it goes while the session holds data, a table read from text, a plot or work in flight (since 2026-11-01, after a reload dropped the 15 plots of a `.plot_all()` unasked): a reload, a closed tab or a typed address gets the browser's `beforeunload` dialog, and a link inside the site or the back button gets the router's blocker with a `confirm`, while the page's own query edits (a library, `&all=1`) and the reload links after `session_expired` or `unreachable` pass (`app/src/hooks/useLeaveWarning.ts`); a request without a readable answer (in production usually an expired Cloudflare Access session) reloads the page once, then offers a reload link; the queue position shows as a small counter and as a `queued · 2 of 3` line above the progress grid; the button adds `source=plot_page` to the URL, which the page reads for `agent_open` and then drops. `app/scripts/agent-bff-mock.mjs` serves the documented BFF routes and a scripted stream locally (for `scatter-basic` and the series family of `line-multi`), so the page can be driven in a browser without the API, the agents service or a model.
- **Analytics** (enum properties only, documented in [Plausible](../reference/plausible.md#agent-chat-admin-only)): the pageview `/debug/agent`, `agent_open{library,source,spec}`, `agent_data_parsed{status,size_bucket}`, `agent_data_confirmed{size_bucket}`, `agent_data_prepared{changes}`, `agent_data_undo{version}`, `agent_plot_rendered{library,status,repaired,spec}`, `agent_guardrail_block{reason}`, `agent_session_saved{include_data}`, `agent_result_feedback{reaction,include_data}`, and `copy_code{page:'agent_chat',method:'agent'}`, all built. The session language fires no event: the chat is admin-only, and the chip's numbers would be tiny.

## Repository layout

```
agents/__init__.py  agents/main.py  agents/Dockerfile  agents/cloudbuild.yaml
agents/anyplot/{__init__.py, agent.py (root_agent, ALL_AGENTS, app = App(...)), models.py, settings.py, policy.py, schemas.py, pipeline.py, run_queue.py, theme_render.py, language.py}
agents/anyplot/sub_agents/{adapter.py, reviewer.py}            # relative imports only: ADK loads the package as top-level `anyplot`
agents/anyplot/tools/{session.py, catalogue.py}
agents/anyplot/plugins/{ledger.py, scope_guard.py, budget.py, tool_safety.py}
agents/anyplot/prompts/{root,adapter,reviewer,scope_judge,data_judge}.md  refusals.yaml
agents/anyplot/data/{parse.py, bindings.py, store.py}           # no ADK import; built; the role parser lives in core/spec_roles.py
agents/anyplot/code/{regions.py, normalise.py, readiness.py, edits.py, validate.py, loader.py, export.py}   # no ADK import; built
agents/anyplot/render/{contract.py, gates.py, png.py, harness.py, serial.py, store.py, watermark.py, runtimes/python.py, backends/{remote.py, local.py, sandbox.py, fake.py}}
agents/anyplot/render/fonts/{JetBrainsMono-Regular.ttf, JetBrainsMono-Bold.ttf, OFL.txt, README.md}   # SIL OFL 1.1, unmodified
agents/renderer/{main.py, executor.py, auth.py, settings.py, wire.py, Dockerfile, cloudbuild.yaml}   # anyplot-renderer; no ADK import, no __init__.py; built
agents/evals/{matrix.py, make_fixtures.py, cases.py, pricing.py, report.py, eligibility.py, scope.py, scope.evalset.json}   # built
agents/evals/{harness/test_flows.py, sync_cases.py,
              baselines/{claude-haiku-5-5,gemini-3.8-flash}.json, fixtures/cases/<id>/{case.json, data.csv} (synthetic only),
              reports/ (gitignored), .cases/ (gitignored, synced from the bucket)}
core/catalogue/{__init__.py, queries.py}      # shared by api/mcp/server.py and agents/
core/canvas.py  core/defects.py                # lifted from impl-review.yml and automation/scripts/regen_gate.py (which keeps a parity-tested copy)
core/database/models.py (AgentFeedback)        alembic/versions/<rev>_agent_feedback.py
api/routers/agent.py                           # BFF, feedback and cases routes
app/src/pages/AgentChatPage.tsx  app/src/sections/agent-chat/{ResultCard,ResultFeedback,...}.tsx  app/src/hooks/useAgentSession.ts  app/src/lib/sse.ts
app/src/pages/DebugPage.tsx (Agent cases section)
tests/unit/agents/*  tests/unit/api/test_agent_router.py  tests/integration/agents/test_pipeline_offline.py
```

`agents/anyplot/code/` exists since 2026-10-09: `validate.py` (the two-profile AST validator), `regions.py` (the AST spans of the THEME block, the Imprint palette, the placeholder, the save calls and the canvas keywords, shared by the other modules; since 2026-10-25 also the save rule per library, `SAVE_RULES`), `normalise.py`, `readiness.py`, `edits.py`, `loader.py` and `export.py`, with tests in `tests/unit/agents/code/`. Their docstrings hold the exact rules. The 13 map specs are the specs tagged both `plot_type: map` and `data_type: geospatial`. `readiness.scan` takes the spec id as a keyword argument, because the map rule needs it and the normalised code no longer carries the catalogue header.

Files to modify: `pyproject.toml` (the extra `agents = ["google-adk==2.11.0"]`; the `agents-eval` extra with `google-adk[eval]` was dropped on 2026-10-12, unused, because its litellm and nltk carried security alerts; setuptools include `agents*`; ruff `known-first-party`; coverage source; regenerate `uv.lock` and run `uv lock --check`; assert that the lock never contains `openai-agents`, which also installs a top-level `agents` module), `.dockerignore` (`!agents`, `!prompts/default-style-guide.md`, `!prompts/library`), `core/config.py` (`agent_*` settings with the pinned defaults, `_strip_secret` entries), `api/routers/debug.py`, `api/main.py`, `api/cloudbuild.yaml` (env vars, secret binding, 401 smoke), `automation/scripts/regen_gate.py` (switch to `core/defects.py`, loaded from a sibling copy in script mode, together with its copy sites), `.github/workflows/{ci-tests,ci-lint,ci-image}.yml`, a new `.github/workflows/agents-eval.yml`, `app/src/routes/{index.tsx,paths.ts}`, `global-config.ts`, `vite-env.d.ts`, `SpecPage.tsx`, `SpecDetailView.tsx`, `DebugPage.tsx`, `app/Dockerfile`, `app/cloudbuild.yaml`, `docs/index.md`, `docs/reference/{api,plausible,mcp,repository,database}.md`, `docs/development.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and `.github/copilot-instructions.md` (an `agents/**` routing row, kept in sync), and one `changelog.d/<slug>.md` per PR. `agents/Dockerfile` builds from `python:3.13-slim` with `uv sync --frozen --extra agents`, runs as a non-root user and starts `uvicorn agents.main:app --port 8080`; its `agents/` copy must include `agents/anyplot/render/fonts/`, because the service loads the footer-strip fonts at start and refuses to start without them, and it copies the catalogue prompt sources `agents/anyplot/policy.py` reads (`prompts/default-style-guide.md`, `prompts/library/`, `prompts/quality-criteria.md` and the two workflow prompts `impl-repair-claude.md` and `ai-quality-review.md`) to their repository paths; `tests/unit/agents/test_image.py` holds that list against every file an import of `agents.main` opens and against the root `.dockerignore`; the plotting extras, the fallback fonts (`/usr/share/fonts/noto`, from a `fonts` build stage that keeps only the allowlisted files of `fonts-noto-core` and `fonts-noto-cjk`) and the baked matplotlib font cache (`/opt/mplconfig`, whose bake fails when a fallback family is missing) belong to the renderer image, `agents/renderer/Dockerfile`, which installs `--extra lib-matplotlib --extra lib-seaborn`, since 2026-10-25 `--extra lib-plotnine --extra lib-altair` and since 2026-10-26 `--extra lib-pygal --extra lib-letsplot`, all without ADK. It links matplotlib's DejaVu Serif and DejaVu Sans Mono into `/usr/share/fonts` for vl-convert's `serif` and `monospace` (altair files that name those families draw no text without them) and, since 2026-10-26, DejaVu Sans next to them, which lets-plot's bridge draws its default text in (it falls back to a Noto Sans face without it). Since 2026-10-26 it also installs Debian's `libcairo2` for cairosvg, which brings `libfontconfig1`, `/etc/fonts/fonts.conf` and Debian's DejaVu core fonts (other bytes under the same names; matplotlib keeps resolving its own copies, and the seaborn, plotnine and altair renders of `ci-image.yml` stayed byte-identical), and copies `agents/renderer/fonts.conf` (one font directory, no cache directory) to `/opt/anyplot/fonts.conf` for lets-plot's row. The ImageMagick linked into lets-plot's bridge is a zero-configuration build that reads no `policy.xml`, so the image installs none of the programs its delegates run (`gs`, `curl`, `soffice`, `ffmpeg` and others), and the image build fails when any of 39 such programs is on the sandbox's `PATH` (`agents/renderer/delegates.py`, which `ci-image.yml` runs again in the built image; since 2026-10-26); since 2026-10-28 the harness's seccomp filter refuses every program start from the bridge whatever is on `PATH`, and every in-process run requires it. It copies the harness to `/opt/anyplot/harness.py`. The `deploy-renderer` trigger (an owner task) fires on `agents/renderer/**`, `agents/anyplot/render/harness.py`, `pyproject.toml` and `uv.lock`; the catalogue's `Project.toml` and `Manifest.toml`, toolchain inputs since 2026-10-29 (the Julia stage precompiles them), are not in its included files yet, an owner task in step 5 of `agentic/specs/261024-agent-all-libraries.md`. The `deploy-agents` trigger (an owner task) fires on `agents/**` except `agents/evals/**`, `core/**`, `prompts/library/**`, `prompts/default-style-guide.md`, `prompts/quality-criteria.md`, `prompts/workflow-prompts/impl-repair-claude.md`, `prompts/workflow-prompts/ai-quality-review.md`, `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock`, `README.md` and `.dockerignore`, so an edit to any prompt source the service sends to the model redeploys it.

Reused code: the `api/mcp/server.py` tool bodies move into `core/catalogue/queries.py`; `core/database/repositories.py` (`SpecRepository.get_by_id_with_code`, `ImplRepository.get_code`); `core/utils.strip_noqa_comments`; `core/constants.py` (`LIBRARIES_METADATA`, `LIBRARY_LANGUAGES`, `INTERACTIVE_LIBRARIES`); `core/palette.py`; the defect grammar through `core/defects.py` and the canvas gate and PNG auto-reject checks through `core/canvas.py`; the render invocation shape of `agentic/workflows/modules/regen/render.py` (copied, not imported); `automation/js-render/render.mjs` for the JS runtime later; the per-IP-hash rate-limit pattern of `api/routers/feedback.py` and `api/request_context.client_ip` for the public phase.

## Roadmap

### Phase 0: local and spikes (about 1 to 1.5 weeks after the API is enabled)

Owner tasks 1 to 6 come first. Then run the spikes:

| Spike | Question | Pass criterion |
|---|---|---|
| M | One call on `eu` and `global` for both arms: `claude-haiku-5-5` through the Anthropic Vertex SDK and gemini-3.8-flash plus gemini-3.5-flash-lite through google-genai (and, for the record, the alias); the reported model version and usage; free `countTokens` on the adapter and reviewer prefixes | Recorded; under $0.01 (done on 2026-10-09: both arms answer on `eu`) |
| S | A Cloud Run sandbox in europe-west4: the deploy works; matplotlib and seaborn render with egress blocked; a blocked fetch fails fast; RSS and time at 2 and 4 vCPU; concurrent sandboxes before OOM; cold start; whether the IAM-forwarded ID-token claims are visible to the container and in what signature state; a probe suite (metadata server, DNS and HTTP egress, `/proc/1/environ`, writes outside `/work`, sudo, fork and memory bombs). Run `gcloud components update` first, because the local SDK 534 lacks the flag; ideally in a throwaway project | Everything blocked or contained |
| G | Can a sandbox read sibling run directories or files the host writes at runtime? | No; otherwise use `--rootfs` with a toolchain-only tree or one render per instance |
| X | Adaptation: 10 specs times matplotlib and seaborn times 6 datasets (renamed, x10, n=12, n=5000, date, decimal comma; the cases are in `agents/evals/fixtures/cases/` and run with `agents/evals/matrix.py`, see `agents/README.md`) on both arms, `claude-haiku-5-5` and gemini-3.8-flash, so the owner picks the phase-1 default by cost and acceptance; plotly only on figure-level gates because the phase-1 image has no Chrome. Pass means all deterministic gates plus an owner blind accept or reject on a sample of 30 renders (`python -m agents.evals.report blind` shows both arms' renders of the same cases without naming the arm). Also reports post-normaliser eligibility under the SECURITY profile and the files blocked for a missing `THEME` block or a non-literal save target (`agents/evals/eligibility.py`, no model call; it sweeps plotnine and altair too since 2026-10-25, and pygal and lets-plot since 2026-10-26) | Under $25 |
| C | Does a NodeTool `Event(message)` arrive before the function response under `StreamingMode.NONE`? | Yes |
| F | Does a plugin's `on_tool_error` catch a `ValueError`? Is the content returned when `before_run` halts emitted? | Both observed |
| A′ | Does the reviewer read a canary string from callback-injected images on a single-turn agent with `include_contents='none'` while its structured output still applies (the native `response_schema` on Gemini, the `output_config.format` on Claude)? | Exact match; fallback is a `view_render` tool returning Parts, which reintroduces the Preview multimodal-function-response path |

Scaffold `agents/`, run `adk web --port 8002 --session_service_uri memory:// --artifact_service_uri memory:// agents` with a development-only `fixture` state seed (snapshot, dataset store and bindings from `agents/evals/fixtures`; refused in production) and the full stack (agents on :8001, the API on :8000, the app on :3000) with `AGENT_RENDERER=local`.

Exit criteria: both arms and the location confirmed by spike M; spikes S and G all blocked with a p95 render under 10 s at 2 vCPU; spike X at least 80 % pass after at most one repair on eligible pairs with owner acceptance of at least 75 % on the sample and a median cost of at most $0.05 per successful plot; scope evals with 100 % of adversarial cases refused and at most 5 % false refusals (guard outages excluded); the validator corpus at 100 % including every named bypass. Spike X against its three lines (2026-10-10): Claude Haiku 5.5 meets all of them, with 83.6 % pass, a median of $0.0041 per passed plot, and the owner's blind acceptance of 28 of 30 renders (93.3 %). Gemini 3.8 Flash meets the acceptance line with 30 of 30 renders (100 %) and misses the other two, with 77.0 % pass and a median of $0.124 per passed plot at list price. Both arms clear the 75 % acceptance line; the defects of the two renders the owner rejected were already named in those runs' residual lines.

Spike X ran both arms on 2026-10-10, on `eu`, with the 122 cases through the throwaway renderer and one run per case.

- **Claude Haiku 5.5** ($0.52): 102 runs passed the gates (83.6 %; 93 of them in runs with two attempts), with a median cost of $0.0041 per passed plot and an end-to-end p50 of 18.5 s. Only 14 runs ended `ok`. 98 ended `needs_attention`, mostly because the probe gate G3 (text beyond the canvas) failed in 60 runs, 56 of which shipped with it, the reviewer's answer could not be read in 18 runs, and the adapter's answer missed its schema in 27 of 235 calls across 23 runs. 10 runs ended `failed (validation)`; 5 of them are `box-basic` in matplotlib, and 4 of those carry `placeholder-count`, where the adapter replaces the `df = load_user_data()` line. 10 of the 20 runs that did not pass shipped with a palette-prefix finding. seaborn passed 88.5 % and matplotlib 78.7 %; the 12-row dataset was the weakest perturbation (70 %) and values times 10 the strongest (95 %).
- **Gemini 3.8 Flash** ($15.89): 94 runs passed the gates (77.0 %), 72 ended `ok`, and all 28 failures are `failed (validation)`, with a median cost of $0.124 per passed plot and an end-to-end p50 of 52.1 s. Read these numbers with a caveat: Gemini counts thinking tokens toward the output cap, so the adapter's 2,048-token edit cap cut off its first answer in every run, and no run had a repair round.
- **Between the arms**, the pass gap is not significant (19 cases flipped one way and 11 the other; exact McNemar test, p ≈ 0.20), and the `ok` rates do not compare plot quality, because each arm's own reviewer decides `ok`.

The run led to these follow-ups: G3 turned out to be a false positive from tick labels that matplotlib never draws, so the probe now measures drawn text only (remote renders, and so the next eval run, pick this up only after the renderer image is rebuilt and deployed); the Gemini adapter's edit cap is 8,192 tokens, and the first attempt keeps 65 s of the soft deadline, enough for a call cut off at that cap; a cut-off answer gets its own repair line; the palette repair target matches the validator's literal-list rule; and the run record names the stage that stopped each run (report schema 2). Of the 50 G3 lines that remain after the fix in the rerun on main, 48 come from three pairs whose shipped `plot.py` is the catalogue original byte for byte, and these are real clips that the originals already carry, as the probe and the saved PNGs show: in heatmap-basic seaborn the x-axis title "Month" ends 23 px below the canvas, in heatmap-basic matplotlib the y-axis title "Department" starts 26 px left of it, and in scatter-basic seaborn the title's ascenders rise 3 px above it, which makes them input for the catalogue normalisation, not for the probe. The other 2 lines come from adapted renders and were not examined: area-basic matplotlib with renamed columns (text reported 55,961 px beyond the canvas edge) and scatter-basic matplotlib with renamed columns (8 px). Such a line is not necessarily a real clip, because the drawn-text probe also measures text that `clip_on=True` keeps off the canvas.

### Phase 1: admin-only production (about 3 to 4 weeks)

1. The design doc (this document).
2. The `search_specs_by_tags` AND and category fix plus the `docs/reference/mcp.md` drift fixes (independent and small).
3. `core/catalogue`, `core/canvas.py`, `core/defects.py`, the `AdminIdentity` refactor and the BFF, shipped dark (owner task 7 before the merge).
4. The `agents/` package, image, Cloud Build config, CI, the regression harness with the spike-X cases as the first fixtures, and the committed baselines of Claude Haiku 5.5 and the Gemini 3.8 Flash arm (owner task 8 before the first deploy).
5. The React page, the button and the analytics events.
6. Quick feedback: the `agent_feedback` migration, the bucket objects, the BFF feedback and cases routes, the `ResultFeedback` control, the DebugPage "Agent cases" section and `sync_cases` (owner task 10 before the merge).
7. `agents-eval.yml` with `workflow_dispatch` inputs, the nightly smoke run on the pinned model including the lifecycle probe, the full suite weekly and on every prompt or model change, plus docs.
8. Optional: sync "What a good version looks like" into `specs.good_version` (Alembic plus `sync_to_postgres.py`) if spike X shows the reviewer flagging expected features.

Exit criteria: 401 or 403 smoke tests on both services; the spend cap active before the first deploy; the log and span canary clean; the owner completes 20 real datasets across 10 specs with at least 75 % accepted through the result button (the `ok` rate reported alongside) and at least 10 of them submitted as feedback cases; for every accepted result the image was copied or downloaded and the copied `plot.py` plus `data.csv` reproduced the shown PNG locally, verified on both viewports and themes; one candidate-model dispatch of the harness produced a diff report; an end-to-end p95 under 60 s; nightly evals green.

### Parallel workstream: catalogue normalisation (pipeline side)

The pipeline owns this workstream, not the agent network: it makes catalogue code data-derived and canvas-correct at the source. [Adaptable catalogue code](adaptable-code.md) defines the convention and the checks that enforce it, and catalogue code converts to that convention through a capped targeted pass over matplotlib and seaborn plus the ordinary daily regeneration, as the rollout in that document describes. Neither the checker nor `impls.adaptation` is built yet. Once they are, `sync_to_postgres.py` computes each implementation's status into `impls.adaptation` at sync time, and the agent's readiness hints come from the same checker instead of the coupling heuristics in `readiness.py`.

### Phase 2: toward public use, all 15 libraries and discovery

Libraries, each one `RuntimeAdapter` enabled through `AGENT_LIBRARIES` and released to the public after its eval matrix reaches 80 %: first plotnine and altair (their runtimes built on 2026-10-25, step 1b of [the phase-2 plan](../../agentic/specs/261024-agent-all-libraries.md)), then lets-plot and pygal (no browser; their runtimes built on 2026-10-26, step 1c), all four served by the deploy since 2026-10-28 for the admin's live tests, while only the admin reaches the chat; then plotly and bokeh (Chromium fixes); then JS through `render.mjs` with `window.ANYPLOT_DATA`, a `page.route` deny-all and a tree-sitter deny-list; then R (ragg and a readr loader); then Julia (a precompiled depot, a pinned `JULIA_CPU_TARGET`, and asynchronous submit-and-poll if renders exceed 60 s; Cloud Run jobs and worker pools also support sandboxes). Discovery follows the phase-2 flow above.

Hard gates before any non-owner use: the legal-page PR (`LegalPage.tsx` plus a pinned test per store), anyplot-api on a dedicated service account, database sessions with a purge, GCS artifacts with a lifecycle, persisted budget counters, Model Armor and `ToolCallIntegrityPlugin`, Turnstile with server-side verification plus a Cloudflare rate rule plus per-IP-hash database quotas plus a signed anonymous identity, routes moved out of `/debug`, and owner decisions on the Vertex 24 h cache and the abuse-logging exception.

## Verification

- **Regression harness.** `uv run --extra agents python -m agents.evals.matrix --model claude-haiku-5-5 --cases smoke --renderer remote --render-url <renderer URL>` must reproduce the committed baseline within tolerance on every PR that touches `agents/anyplot/prompts/`, `code/` or `render/` (run through `workflow_dispatch`, not automatically, because it spends money). A candidate run with `--model <new>` prints the diff table and exits non-zero on regressions.
- **Evals.** The scope set is judge-only (`uv run --extra agents python -m agents.evals.scope`, built and first run on 2026-10-10): it sends the 205 synthetic cases of `agents/evals/scope.evalset.json` to the judge the settings name, with the same context the ScopeGuard builds from each case's earlier turns (a fixed refusal among them is left out of the last assistant turn, as the plugin leaves out the one the halt event stores), without ADK, the root or a render, for about $0.05 a run. It paces the judge calls at 25 a minute by default (`--calls-per-minute`), so a run takes about 7 minutes: the project's Vertex AI quota for Claude Haiku in `eu` was 30 requests per minute until 2026-10-31 (150 since), failed calls included, and unpaced runs hit HTTP 429 after about 60 cases; a case the judge could not answer keeps the cause, the failure's exception type. The cases are 83 in scope (since 2026-10-14 seven questions about the user's own data, tagged `data_question`, three of them right after a `needs_data` refusal, the live sequence in German and English; four of them with personal data: payee names, an address column, an e-mail address in a byline, and a payment reminder on the user's own tax plot, which must not read as a call to action; since 2026-10-11 six requests to draw a formula or a curve, three English and three German, tagged `formula`, which the assistant computes and never fetches; since 2026-10-12 six requests to write the plot's own text in another language, three English and three German, tagged `plot_language`, which are in scope, unlike a translation for use outside the plot; since 2026-10-14 six sets of notes pasted with a table, tagged `notes` and judged inside `<user_notes>` the way the dataset route judges them: a source line with a web address and a license, an export's metadata, footnotes and a unit, a plot wish and a German station note), 53 off-topic and 69 adversarial (German, role play, base64 and other encodings, multi-turn drift, general-programming requests framed as plot-code questions, mixed and plot-framed requests, advertisements and calls to action in plot text, and requests for data the service would have to fetch, a plain fact question included and, since 2026-10-14, "do it anyway" in German and English after a `needs_data` refusal, and since 2026-10-14 an injection hidden among the notes of a pasted table, which must be an `attack`); each has `text`, `last_turns`, `expected` (`in_scope`, `out_of_scope`, `attack` or `needs_data`), `lang` and `tags`. It reports adversarial recall, the false-refusal rate, refusal recall, the exact and `needs_data` exact rates, a confusion matrix and a per-tag table, and exits non-zero below 100 % adversarial recall or above 5 % false refusals; a `--tags` subset gates only the metrics its cases measure and writes its own report file. The first run (2026-10-10, Claude Haiku 5.5 in `eu`, $0.054, on the 176 cases the set held then) passed both gates: 100 % adversarial recall, no false refusal and 98.8 % exact verdicts. Two attacks framed as plot-code questions got `out_of_scope` instead of `attack`, the same refusal without a strike, and two base64 cases and a cipher case got no verdict because the judge's answer failed its schema twice, which the service turns into a blocking `guard_unavailable`. This eval measures the judge only; the output guard's reply cap has its own case set in the unit tests (a long customer mail, a long Japanese essay and a list of sixty items that must be refused; change lists, how-to-run steps and five-sentence replies in English, German, French, Spanish, Italian, Portuguese, Polish, Japanese and Chinese that must pass). Not built: a second stage that sends the adversarial cases the judge passes through the real root and checks that the reply equals the fixed refusal. The flow evals (`use_with_my_data`, `injection` through CSV cells, headers, image labels and catalogue-style comments, and `code_escape`) run as a pytest harness against the real `/v1` routes in-process with fixture CSVs, because the pipeline reads the snapshot, dataset and bindings from server-set state; trajectory, `token_usage_v1` and `inference_call_count_v1` are tracked as regressions. A full eval run costs about $1 to $3.
- **Unit tests on every PR.** The parser (decimal comma with `DD.MM.YYYY`, header canonicalisation, bidi and zero-width names, caps); the bindings heuristic and `bindings.apply`; the edit applier (ambiguity, protected regions by AST, palette prefix); the validator allow and deny corpus for both profiles (dunder walks, `__builtins__`, `string.Formatter`, `.format`, patsy, `pd.eval`, file-read calls); loader substitution and the working and run forms; the canvas normaliser on the 137 legacy matplotlib files; gate fixtures (G10's from the harness's own glyph capture, which a test exercises with DejaVu alone); the stream translator (author filter, dropped function calls, code-block stripping); the plugins with fake clients (judge timeout or garbage gives `guard_unavailable`; the budget halts the root only; tool errors are mapped; one `plot_pipeline` per invocation); the agent registry test; the BFF (401 and 403, the CSRF header, the 404 kill switch and unset key, user-id derivation, the body allowlist, ownership on artifact routes, SSE re-framing); the caller-claims check; a log and span canary; `find_specs` AND semantics; the session language (English by default whatever the locale, detection by the first in-scope typed message only and the three-word rule for an `en` verdict, a choice that detection never overrides, `set_language` and the language route validated alike, `409 run_active`, the `done` field, the fenced value in every request); Vitest for the SSE parser, the page, the result card, the language chip and button gating.
- **Run locally.**

  ```bash
  uv sync --extra agents --extra lib-matplotlib --extra lib-seaborn --extra lib-plotnine --extra lib-altair --extra lib-pygal --extra lib-letsplot --extra test
  gcloud auth application-default login
  export ENVIRONMENT=development GOOGLE_GENAI_USE_ENTERPRISE=TRUE GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT=anyplot GOOGLE_CLOUD_LOCATION=eu \
         AGENT_LOCATION=eu AGENT_PROVIDER=anthropic-vertex AGENT_MODEL=claude-haiku-5-5 \
         AGENT_JUDGE_MODEL=claude-haiku-5-5 AGENT_RENDERER=local
  uv run adk web agents --port 8002 --session_service_uri memory:// --artifact_service_uri memory://
  uv run uvicorn agents.main:app --port 8001
  AGENT_ENABLED=true AGENT_SERVICE_URL=http://localhost:8001 AGENT_USER_ID_KEY=dev-key ADMIN_TOKEN=<token> \
    uv run uvicorn api.main:app --reload --port 8000
  cd app && VITE_ENABLE_AGENT_CHAT=true yarn dev   # enter <token> on /debug, then open /debug/agent?spec=scatter-basic&library=matplotlib
  ```

  For the Gemini arm, export `AGENT_PROVIDER=gemini AGENT_MODEL=gemini-3.8-flash AGENT_JUDGE_MODEL=gemini-3.5-flash-lite` instead. `agents/README.md` has the step-by-step version, including `ADK_DISABLE_LOAD_DOTENV=1` for `adk web`.

- **Measure per run.** Status and reason, the stage that stopped the run, attempts, gate failures by gate, verdict, owner accept or reject, LLM calls, `usage_metadata` (prompt, candidates, thoughts, cached), `model_version`, cost, time in the run queue and how the wait ended (the `queue` attribution line), time to first event, end-to-end time, render wall time per theme, theme toggles, and refusal, `guard_unavailable`, queue-full and budget counts.

## Owner tasks

These are manual steps only the owner can do; they are tracked in Todoist.

1. Enable `aiplatform.googleapis.com` in project `anyplot` and authorize spike M (under $0.01).
2. Create the service account `anyplot-agents@` with `roles/aiplatform.user` and `roles/telemetry.writer`; grant `roles/iam.serviceAccountUser` and `roles/iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator` on it to the Cloud Build identity. Done on 2026-10-11: the account holds `roles/aiplatform.user` and `roles/telemetry.writer`, and the Compute Engine default service account (the Cloud Build identity) holds both grants on it. `roles/run.invoker` on the service followed in task 8.
3. Create a spend-cap budget on `aiplatform.googleapis.com` sized at list price (the promo is credited back later): about CHF 75 for phase 0, or run spike X in a throwaway project under its own cap; about CHF 60 in steady state (owner usage plus eval cost with 2x headroom); alerts at 50, 80 and 100 %. Never cap `run.googleapis.com`.
4. Authorize spike S (the Cloud Run sandbox, ideally in a throwaway project, after `gcloud components update`).
5. Authorize spike X (under $25).
6. Confirm that Cloudflare Access `anyplot.ai/debug*` and `/api/debug*` cover `/debug/agent` and `/api/debug/agent/*`, and name the Cloudflare plan (the Free plan allows one rate-limit rule).
7. Done on 2026-10-11: the Secret Manager secret `AGENT_USER_ID_KEY` exists (44 printable characters, the base64 of 32 random bytes, written through `--data-file`, never `echo`; the value has to stay printable, because the deploy injects it as an environment variable), the anyplot-api runtime service account reads it, and `api/cloudbuild.yaml` binds it. The deploy's smoke calls `/debug/agent/status` with the admin token and expects `200` while `_AGENT_ENABLED` is true.
8. Done on 2026-10-11, the API trigger's `_AGENT_SERVICE_URL` and `_AGENT_ENABLED=true` included (the deploy-app trigger sets `_VITE_ENABLE_AGENT_CHAT=true` as well): the service runs at `https://anyplot-agents-r3tvmejsmq-ez.a.run.app` and the `deploy-agents` trigger is enabled with `_RENDER_URL`. The recipe, for the record: run the first build of `agents/cloudbuild.yaml` as the Compute Engine default service account (`--service-account`, because a manual submit may otherwise run as the legacy Cloud Build account, which the grants do not cover) with `_SMOKE_AUTH=false` and `_RENDER_URL` (the renderer's `status.url`; the deploy refuses to run without it), which creates the service without `--no-traffic`; grant `roles/run.invoker` on `anyplot-agents` to the API's runtime identity and to `anyplot-agents@`; rebuild with the token smoke (`_SMOKE_AUTH=true`); create the `deploy-agents` trigger with that `_RENDER_URL`, running as the same account; and set the API trigger's `_AGENT_SERVICE_URL` to the service's `status.url`. The exact commands are in the header of `agents/cloudbuild.yaml`.
9. Before PR 7: grant `roles/aiplatform.user` to the GitHub Workload Identity Federation principal for this repository, conditioned on `agents-eval.yml` on `main` if possible.
10. Before PR 6 merges: create the private EU bucket `anyplot-agent-cases` (uniform access, public-access prevention, lifecycle delete after 180 days) and confirm that the anyplot-api runtime identity can write to it.
11. Confirm the GCP agreement type: the online terms mean abuse-monitoring prompt logging for up to 90 days; a Master Agreement is exempt.
12. Decide the fate of issue #5405, which proposed Claude with Find, Navigate and Create modes: supersede it with this document or update it. Register the Plausible properties and goals.
13. Before public use: move `anyplot-api` off the default compute service account, which holds `roles/editor`.
14. Done on 2026-10-11: the renderer runs at `https://anyplot-renderer-r3tvmejsmq-ez.a.run.app` and the `deploy-renderer` trigger is enabled with the caller list, the audiences and the smoke render. The recipe, for the record: create the service account `anyplot-renderer@` with no role; grant `roles/iam.serviceAccountUser` and `roles/iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator` on it to the Cloud Build identity; run the first build of `agents/renderer/cloudbuild.yaml` with `_SMOKE_RENDER=false`; grant `roles/run.invoker` on `anyplot-renderer` to `anyplot-agents@`, `anyplot-renderer@` itself and the owner's account; rebuild with `_ALLOWED_CALLERS` (`anyplot-agents@`, `anyplot-renderer@`, the owner's account), `_AUDIENCES` (the OAuth client ids of the owner's user tokens; the deploy adds the service URL itself, and Cloud Run accepts no tag URL as an audience) and `_SMOKE_RENDER=true`; then create the `deploy-renderer` trigger with those substitutions.

## Open decisions

Defaults apply until the owner decides otherwise.

- DQ-02 topics (politics, religion, violence) in user data: allowed as data, labels only.
- The Highcharts commercial licence for user plots (phase 2).
- An attribution header in exported code: yes.
- A footer strip on the served PNGs: decided on 2026-10-10, yes, at image level only (variant `s1c-jb-sq2`: JetBrains Mono, the dot as a brand-green square); the exported code stays without it, and `AGENT_WATERMARK=false` switches it off. See [Footer strip](#footer-strip).
- Disabling the Vertex 24 h in-memory cache (needs `roles/aiplatform.admin` and applies to all regions): no in phase 1.
- Retention periods for the legal page (phase 2).
- Scale-to-zero "session expired" versus `min-instances=1`: accept in phase 1.
- A named fallback model on `eu` for capacity failover, which deviates from one pinned model everywhere: none.
- The request timeout for a queued turn: a run at the back of a full queue needs up to 780 s (600 s in the queue, 180 s to run). Decided on 2026-10-10: anyplot-api runs with `--timeout=900` and the BFF caps a turn at `AGENT_TURN_MAX_S` (890 s), so the full wait is reachable through the BFF. Raised on 2026-10-13 with the 540 s deadline (owner: a complicated plot must not stop at 80 %): up to 1,140 s, `--timeout=1200` on both services and a 1,190 s turn cap.
- The queue length: the owner's formula (rate x maximum wait / 60) assumes runs within the 60 s rate window, so with longer runs the back of a full queue waits the full maximum and gets `capacity`. Decided on 2026-10-10: the formula stays; admitting by an estimated start time (position times the larger of 60 s per start and the recent run time, divided by the concurrency) is the fallback if the live service shows many expiries.
- One run or toggle in flight per user, across sessions (stricter than refusing a toggle only while that session runs): decided on 2026-10-10, kept.
- Whether the "Create plot" action carries the site theme, so a dark-mode visitor's first render is already dark instead of a light render plus a toggle: not yet; the action renders light.
- Feedback-case retention and data inclusion: 180 days in the private bucket; `include_data` on by default for admins and off by default with explicit consent for real users.
- The sandbox's private `/tmp` and memory: spike S2 counts each sandbox's `/tmp` tmpfs (about 1.9 GiB, no flag to shrink it) as instance memory, which on paper puts even one sandbox (1 GiB address space, 1.9 GiB `/tmp`, the 512 MiB volume) above the 3 GiB budget of a 4 GiB instance. Measured on 2026-10-10 through the deployed renderer (a throwaway service, 2 vCPU, 4 GiB, one sandbox at a time): code that writes 40 MiB files into `/tmp` until it is full ends after 4.6 s with `OSError: [Errno 28] No space left on device`, the instance stays up with 3,328 MiB available afterwards (3,451 before), and the memory watchdog never has to fire. Decided: a 4 GiB instance with one sandbox at a time, without `RENDERER_BIND_TMP` (which stays available, untested) and without an 8 GiB instance; the validator (no file writes in user code), the serial slot, the `MemAvailable` floor before a render (1,024 MiB) and the watchdog's floor during one (512 MiB, reason `memory`) stay as the controls. Re-measure before a second render slot.
- How a developer's `adk web` authenticates to the deployed renderer: `AGENT_RENDER_TOKEN` from `gcloud auth print-identity-token` is the path spike S proved against Cloud Run IAM; the token minted from a user's Application Default Credentials carries the ADC OAuth client id as `aud`, and whether Cloud Run IAM accepts it is not yet verified. Either way, the token's client id joins `RENDERER_AUDIENCES` (both client ids are in the deployed service's `_AUDIENCES`) and the owner's email `RENDERER_ALLOWED_CALLERS`. Checked on 2026-10-11 against the deployed renderer: the gcloud CLI token renders (the harness and the local stack use it); the ADC token path is still unverified.
- Cold starts of the renderer: with `--min-instances=0`, the first render after an idle period meets a cold instance, and spike S saw the front end answer 500, then 429, and a 200 only about 17 s after the first request. The `remote` backend retries such answers after 1, 3, 8 and 15 s within 30 s. The deployed renderer runs with `--min-instances=0` since 2026-10-11, and the harness rendered through it that day without a retry failure, but no render has been timed against a deliberately cold instance yet; that measurement decides whether the retries suffice or the renderer runs with `_MIN_INSTANCES=1` (the billing commitments probably absorb its cost).
- What a requested text or number format that the render does not show does (`AGENT_EXPECT_CHECK`): `repair` (the default since 2026-10-15: a measured finding that gets a repair round, about 40 s more) or `note` (only the residual line, so the reply says the plot does not show it yet and offers another try).
- Premium positioning: [Vision](vision.md) lists "Try with your data" as premium; the licensing row in the decisions table settles that the code stays MIT regardless.

## Risks and mitigations

| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| Cloud Run sandboxes are in Preview with undocumented regions, isolation technology and cross-run visibility | Spikes S and G plus the probe suite gate phase 1; the `RenderBackend` protocol; the fallback is a gen2 service with Direct VPC egress deny or `GkeCodeExecutor` with a custom image; the sandboxes run in `anyplot-renderer`, a service of its own with a zero-role account, from phase 1 |
| The model adapts poorly (spike X: 83.6 % of the Claude arm's runs and 77.0 % of the Gemini arm's pass the gates, but only 14 of Claude's 122 runs end `ok`; unreadable reviewer answers, adapter schema misses and the palette rule are the first prompt and validator items, and the G3 clipping gate was a probe false positive, fixed in the probe; remote renders get the fix once the renderer image is rebuilt) | Spike X is the go or no-go with owner blind acceptance; an eligibility filter (statically clean data-generation pairs: matplotlib 65 of 251, seaborn 84 of 267; the dpi normaliser removes the canvas condition; plotly's 102 count only in phase 2); hints from the readiness scan; the normalisation workstream; an honest `needs_attention` status |
| Gemini 3.8 Flash, the second arm, is a short-term tier not on the tier list, and `eu` has its own capacity pool | One `AGENT_MODEL` value, `retry_options` with backoff from day one, `error{code:"capacity"}` on persistent 429s, no fallback model unless the owner names one, the lifecycle probe, an eval gate on every bump |
| The Vertex quota for Claude Haiku in `eu` was 30 requests per minute in the project `anyplot` until 2026-10-31 (an override; Google's default is 1,500), failed calls included, and the service does not pace its judge calls: a wide dataset of 20 to 30 judge parts, four at a time, spent most of a minute's quota within seconds, after which the next upload or message failed closed with `guard_unavailable` until the minute passed | Eased: on 2026-10-31 the owner raised the overrides to 150 requests, 1,000,000 input tokens and 200,000 output tokens per minute, so a wide dataset takes a fifth of a minute's requests. Not built: a process-wide judge rate limit, which would make a wide upload wait; failing closed keeps it safe meanwhile. The scope eval paces itself at 25 calls per minute (`--calls-per-minute`), and the judge's retry waits 0.5 s |
| The reviewer is a weak judge (expert correlation about 0.43) | Deterministic gates first, a short checklist, at most one repair by default (`AGENT_MAX_ATTEMPTS`), residual defects shown as notes, owner acceptance as the real metric |
| ADK ships weekly with feature-flag drift | An exact pin to 2.11.0; re-check `FUNCTION_TOOL_ARG_VALIDATION` and `JSON_SCHEMA_FOR_FUNC_DECL` on upgrade; plain-code modules without ADK imports; the harness report stamps the ADK version |
| Caller trust: anyplot-api runs as the shared compute service account | Acceptable while admin-only; the IAM-forwarded claims check; a dedicated service account before public use |
| The fan-out's data copies (`/dataset/copy`) count as no paste, and each one takes the dataset's full size in the shared 256 MiB store again | Acceptable while admin-only: a fan-out session takes one copy, and a user at most 30 a minute and 150 a day (`COPIES_PER_MINUTE`, `COPIES_PER_DAY`, review of 2026-11-01, about ten fan-outs a day); a per-user byte budget in the dataset store before public use |
| Deadline overrun (2 renders of one theme times 60 s plus LLM calls) | A 480 s soft deadline inside the pipeline (140 s before 2026-10-13, which skipped the repair in 11 of 20 measured runs; skip the repair when less than 75 s are left, 60 s for its adapter call and 15 s for its render; clamp the render timeout); the Gemini adapter's 8,192-token edit cap, at which a cut-off first call takes about 58 s at spike X's fitted rate, so a cut-off still leaves the repair its 75 s (at 10,240 such a call would take about 72 s and cancel the repair; in exchange, a plan above 8,192 tokens loses its own repair round and comes back on attempt 2), a `try/finally` that always yields a `PlotResult`, the 540 s `abort_signal` backstop |
| Long SSE streams hold anyplot-api concurrency slots on its single instance | The 540 s deadline, the 1,190 s turn cap, one run per user, and a queue of at most 10 waiting streams; the route moves out of the API before public use |
| A queued turn outlasts a request timeout on its path: the 600 s maximum wait plus the 540 s deadline is 1,140 s, plus the root's first call, since the wait happens mid-turn (2026-10-14) | anyplot-api runs with `--timeout=1200`; the BFF restarts its turn budget while the run waits but never past `AGENT_TURN_MAX_S` (1,190 s): a turn still queued when its run could no longer finish inside the cap ends with `error{code:"capacity"}` and leaves the queue before it spends a token, so Cloud Run never cuts a stream silently |
| Out of memory with more than one sandbox (spikes S and S2) | One run in flight; serial renders by construction, because `SerialRenderer` in `Services.backend` puts every backend and every route (the theme toggle and `adk web` included) behind the same slots, so a backend cannot forget its semaphore |
| Theme toggles crowd out paid runs (renders that cost no tokens but hold the one render slot) | A freed render slot goes to a waiting pipeline render first; one run or toggle in flight per user, and a toggle and a turn refuse each other in a session; a toggle waits for a slot at most 120 s; a theme that failed the host gates is rendered at most twice |
| Cloudflare 524 or Worker buffering | The generator route with automatic 15 s pings and first bytes sent immediately, verified in the phase-1 smoke test; asynchronous polling for slow runtimes later |
| A label in a script the image's fonts do not cover, or an emoji outside Noto Sans Symbols2 and DejaVu Sans (🔥, a flag), draws as placeholder boxes and the user reads the feature as broken | The harness records matplotlib's missing-glyph reports; G10 names the count and the characters to the repair and keeps the line in the residual lines after a reviewer `ok`; the CI image job renders seven scripts and a DejaVu-only control on every renderer change |
| Hidden CDN dependencies (bokeh, kaleido MathJax, map tiles) | The exclusion list of 13 map specs, inline and offline settings, the non-blank gate |
| Retiring defaults (`Gemini()` and the eval judge default to 2.5 Flash) | Always set `model`; the registry test asserts that every model string comes from settings |
| The spend cap trips on list price or on evals | A cap sized at list price with eval cost included; nightly smoke only; spike X in a throwaway project |
| The privacy page contradicts non-owner use | The owner-only gate; the legal PR and the retention design are hard gates |

## Evidence

The design rests on research that was fact-checked on 2026-10-08 against the ADK v2.11.0 source tree, the ADK documentation at adk.dev, the Vertex AI (Gemini Enterprise Agent Platform) model, location, pricing and data-governance pages, the Cloud Run sandbox documentation, and this repository. Measured repository facts (canvas failures, literal coupling, data-swap pass rates, prompt and implementation token sizes, catalogue index sizes) come from read-only scans of `plots/`, `prompts/` and the metadata YAML files. Re-verify model ids, prices, regional availability and ADK API names before implementing a section, because all four change on a weekly to monthly cadence.
